  from `parseAffectedOptions` with the AFP-V0-436 default.
- **AFP-V0-438:** (proposed, experimental; V1-2572, GH #1601)
  A default location that cannot be used MUST NOT be an error: when `os.UserCacheDir` fails or is
  not absolute, when the location fails any AFP-V0-390 placement rule (inside the root, a `.git`
  component, the Git or common directory, or a Git directory that cannot be named), or when it
  cannot be created, opened, kept private or written, the call runs uncached, writes nothing to
  stderr and exits as `--no-cache` would. The receipt MUST be byte-identical with `--no-cache`, a
  cold default cache and a warm default cache (AFP-V0-393), for every plan form. Rollback: as
  AFP-V0-436.
- **AFP-V0-439:** (proposed, experimental; V1-2572, GH #1601)
  `corvint [--root PATH] affected cache [--format json|doctor-plugin]` MUST report the default
  location for the root without creating, opening an entry of or writing anything: one canonical
  JSON object, profile `affected-cache-status/0`, with exactly `bytes` (the sum of regular file
  sizes in the directory), `entries` (files named `affected-graph-*.json`), `location` (resolved,
  or empty when there is none), `mutates` (false), `ok` (true), `profile`, `reason` and `state`:
  `PRESENT`, `ABSENT` (not yet created), `UNAVAILABLE` (no user cache directory, a placement the
  AFP-V0-390 rules refuse, or a directory the planner's handle and privacy checks refuse, such
  as one writable by another user, so plan calls run uncached) or
  `INCOMPLETE` (the listing stopped at its 4096-entry bound or failed). The directory is opened
  nonblocking and held to a directory by its descriptor, so a FIFO cannot hang the call, and is
  read in fixed batches without descending. `--format doctor-plugin` prints the TQD-V0-010 plugin
  array with exactly one `AFFECTED_CACHE` item whose `who` is the location (`affected-cache`
  when there is none) and whose `detail` names the state, entries and bytes, so
  `corvint-tasks doctor --plugins DIR` can show the cache; there is no `corvint doctor` command.
  It exits 0 in every state and 2 (`invalid-arguments`) for any other argument or a repeated or
  unknown `--format`. Rollback: drop the `cache` dispatch and `runAffectedCacheStatus`.
- **AFP-V0-441:** (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11; V1-2682); its
  `.github` change under the owner's `ci-control-plane` OK, owner chat 2026-10-25) In
  `go-product-shard` only, and only on a `pull_request` or `merge_group` run whose outcomes were
  captured, the AFP-V0-047 rerun SHALL be the shard's one in-job retry. The rerun of all named
  invocations together SHALL run under its own hang detector, `timeout -k 1m` of `RERUN_MINUTES`
  (15) minutes, exit 124 when it expires (137 when its kill escalation stops it), and record its
  exit status in `rerun-status`; a failed whole-package invocation of the first attempt SHALL leave
  `whole-failed`. The failed shard SHALL pass only when its outcomes are complete and
  `.github/forward-rows/flaky.sh` finds that the first attempt failed, `rerun.state` is `RAN` with
  at least one invocation, the rerun exited 0, every rerun outcome is `pass`, every `fail` or
  `missing` package of the first attempt was rerun, each package-level `fail` event of the first
  stream has its own rerun invocation of that package (so a lost `failed-slices` record cannot
  pass), and a failed whole-package invocation named at least one whole package; otherwise it prints `AFP-V0-441: not FLAKY: <reason>` and the
  shard keeps its first-attempt failure, so a second failure, a rerun timeout, a bounded
  (`SKIPPED_BOUND`) or interrupted rerun and a push or uncaptured run all fail the job and its
  required check. A FLAKY shard SHALL append a `FLAKY` section naming the first attempt's failed
  tests (or packages when no test failed) to the job summary, print a `::warning` annotation
  titled `FLAKY go-product-shard <shard>` naming up to 20 of them, set the step output `flaky=1`
  (a failed write keeps the failure), set no `costs` output, and retain `ci-flaky-<event>-<shard>`
  for 90 days in a step whose failed or empty upload fails the shard: `flaky.json`, a
  `corvint-ci-flaky/0` document with exactly `profile`, `verdict` (`FLAKY`), `event`, `runId`,
  `runAttempt`, `target`, `shard`, `shards`, `firstStatus`, `rerunInvocations`, `failedPackages`
  and `failedTests` (`package`, `test`). The outcomes document and its first-attempt `status` are
  unchanged, so forward rows still see the failure. Doc, drift and other gates never retry. Limits:
  a weekly flake rate is computable from the retained artifacts but not reported; a FLAKY pass
  makes the run successful for consumers that read run conclusions. Rollback: drop the
  `flaky.sh` call, `RERUN_MINUTES`, the timeout wrapper and the `ci-flaky` upload; the shard then
  fails after the rerun as before.

### Ignored untracked files in path mode (AFP-V0-440, GitHub #1774)

- **AFP-V0-440:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2674, GitHub #1774)
  Amending AFP-V0-094, a `--path` plan MUST NOT refuse only because a TypeScript unit lists a
  file that Git ignores and HEAD does not track, such as a gitignored
  `playwright-report/trace/assets/*.js` bundle. When, after the graph build, a TypeScript unit
  lists such a file and no other plugin's unit lists one, the graph MUST be built again,
  uncached, with the `TJAA-V0-100` exclusion held on the root, so that file is no graph input,
  enters no receipt and raises no frontier, the rule a `--playwright-config` plan already
  follows. The rebuilt graph MUST be used only when its canonical form, with every TypeScript
  unit, frontier reason and unobserved entry removed, equals the first graph's: only the
  TypeScript adapter turns an import of an excluded file into an unresolved import, so any other
  change, such as an ignored `.go` file the Go adapter could not parse or a Python import that
  would silently resolve nowhere, keeps the refusal over the first graph. Every other AFP-V0-094
  refusal MUST hold over the graph used: a listed ignored path; a unit of another plugin that
  lists one (an ignored `.go` file is compiled with its package); an ignored
  entry overlapping a Go unit's `testdata/`, embedding or read-scoped directory or declared read
  scope; and an entry the exclusion drops (U+FFFD or no valid relative path). A dirty worktree,
  including an untracked file Git does not ignore and a modified tracked file, still refuses
  before the graph walk. A change in the ignored list between its first read and the end of the
  compile MUST refuse with `unsupported-affected-drift`. The rebuild runs only in the case that
  refused before, so no plan that succeeded before changes or loses its cache. The default and
  `--base` forms without `--playwright-config` are unchanged.
  Failure modes: an import that resolves only to an excluded file is unresolved (`TJAA-V0-100`),
  so the plan's scope widens rather than narrows. Rollback: drop `affectedPathListGraph`,
  `affectedGraphListsIgnored` and `affectedGraphsAgreeOutsideTypeScript` and call `affectedPathListIgnoredCheck` over the first graph again;
  no persisted store or receipt format changes.

### Playwright declaration closure and the declaration path (AFP-V0-442, AFP-V0-443, GitHub #1751)

Intent (GitHub #1751 retest, V1-2693): a repository whose standalone Node CLI (`scripts/*.mjs`)
reads a declared documentation file got `playwright:declared-path-contradicted`, although no
Playwright test loads that script, and a branch that adds `.corvint/documentation-paths.json`
raised `UNOWNED_DIRTY_PATH` for the declaration itself. Both kept a bounded plan from narrowing.

- **AFP-V0-442:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2693, GitHub #1751)
  Amending `TJAA-V0-101` (2), only a source in the Playwright closure contradicts the
  documentation declaration. There are four parts.
  - (1) The closure is the config's unit, every unit holding a bound Playwright test, and every
    unit those reach by an import, test import, advisory import, route-dispatch import or exec
    edge, including the global hooks the config binds. To a fixpoint, it also holds every unit a
    change of which reaches a closure unit (as importer or path-literal reader, such as a spec
    that names a script as an argument), with that unit's own edges. It is incomplete when more
    than 2,048 units lie outside the edge closure, when a closure unit carries a unit frontier
    or dropped its path tokens at the bound, or when a graph frontier reason has no located site
    or a site whose file is in the closure or is no graph unit.
  - (2) A file that names a matched path is in the closure, and contradicts the declaration as
    `TJAA-V0-101` (3) says, when it is the config, is no TypeScript graph unit (for example
    `jsconfig.json`), the closure is incomplete, or its unit is in the closure. The unknown's
    detail adds that reason. Any file whose membership is unknown therefore still refuses. The
    closure is built only once some file names a matched path.
  - (3) Every other file that names a matched path is an out-of-closure reader. The plan's
    `declaration` member lists each one, in path order, in `outOfClosure` as `path` (the first
    matched path it names) and `site` (file, line and spelling); the member is omitted when there
    are none. Such a reader alone leaves `state` `APPLIED`.
  - (4) A project-owned list of tool-only readers is a non-goal: the closure classifies a reader
    from observed edges, while an asserted exemption would let declared authority override
    observed evidence that disagrees with it (`TJAA-V0-101` (3)).
  Non-goals: modelling a reader that a test reaches only through a spawned process whose command
  no closure unit names (a `package.json` script, a `webServer` command), which is the bounded
  exclusion `TJAA-V0-013` already keeps for helpers no spec reaches; modelling reporters, which
  the closure does not include. Failure modes: an incomplete closure or an unowned reader refuses,
  so the plan widens to the full suite rather than narrows. Rollback: drop `playwrightClosure` and
  the `outOfClosure` member and count every observed file as `TJAA-V0-101` (2) did; no persisted
  store changes.
- **AFP-V0-443:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2693, GitHub #1751)
  Extending `AFP-V0-160`, a dirty `.corvint/documentation-paths.json` that no unit names and no
  dirty-path walk reaches (no path literal, declared read scope, directory reader or unbounded
  reader) MUST raise no `UNOWNED_DIRTY_PATH` and MUST be listed in `nonSource` as one
  `CORVINT_DECLARATION` group, after the `DECLARED_DOCUMENTATION` group: the affected planner
  reads it, classifies the plan's paths by its current bytes and records its digest, so it is no
  build or test input. A unit that names or reaches it keeps the unknown entry, so in a
  repository whose own code reads the declaration, such as Corvint, the path stays unowned.
  Failure modes: a reader that resolves the path at run time from parts no token holds is not
  seen, as for `AFP-V0-088`. Rollback: drop `declarationPath` and `ClassCorvintDeclaration`.

### Over-bound sources, generated paths and the selection audit (AFP-V0-444, AFP-V0-445, AFP-V0-447; GitHub #1811, #1814)

- **AFP-V0-444:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2702, GitHub #1811)
  A Playwright source walk (`--playwright-config`, `affected discovery`, and the e2e-safe
  profile's discovery) MUST NOT fail the whole run because one file is over the fixed
  `MaxSourceBytes` (4,194,304-byte) per-file bound. (1) The TypeScript observer MUST record such a
  file as the frontier site `typescript:over-bound-source:<path>` instead of an unreadable source;
  the Playwright source digest MUST still bind it, by a streamed SHA-256 of at most 1 GiB marked
  `over_bound`, and every existing digest without such a file is unchanged. (2) A repository MAY
  declare generated or vendored paths in `.corvint/generated-paths.json`
  (`{"profile":"corvint-generated-paths/0","paths":[...]}`, the AFP-V0-160 (1) entry rules: a
  repository-relative file, or a directory ending in `/`, strictly ascending, at most 256, none
  under `.corvint/`, at most 64 KiB). Every walk of those three commands MUST exclude a declared
  path, as it does a gitignored one, and the Playwright plan MUST record the declaration as
  `plan.generated` `{path, paths, sha256}` so the exclusion is visible and bound; an invalid or
  unreadable declaration refuses with `unsupported-affected-status`, never as an empty one.
  (3) Every over-bound read error MUST name the fixed bound, and the Playwright plan's over-bound
  unknown and the 1 GiB identity refusal MUST also name the remedy: the bound is fixed and cannot
  be raised; listing the path in the declaration is the way past it. A walk of those commands MUST
  hold the declaration as well as filter by it, and a change of Git's ignored list or the declared
  entries while they compile MUST refuse with `unsupported-affected-drift`. (4) Under `--selection-profile e2e-safe`, an over-bound site MUST make the closure
  unbounded only when the change touches that path (or the site is unnamed or folded into `+N`);
  a change that does not touch it still narrows. A `--playwright-config` plan with an undeclared
  over-bound file stays `NOT_NARROWED` (`GRAPH_FRONTIER`), because the unread file could be a
  test; the declaration is the remedy there. Amended by `TJAA-V0-108`/`TJAA-V0-109` (GitHub #1878):
  only an over-bound file that can change the Playwright answer (dirty, a file a project may load
  as a test, or one a closure or dirty-reached unit may name) keeps that widening; any other is
  listed in `plan.informational` with the remedy and does not widen.
  Non-goals: a configurable per-file bound; parsing an over-bound file; using the declaration for
  the Go, Python or other adapters. Failure modes: a declared path that is really hand-written
  source drops its imports from the graph, so a change to it is unmapped and widens the plan; an
  over-bound file larger than 1 GiB keeps the whole-run refusal, naming the remedy. Rollback:
  delete `internal/liveverify/affected/generated.go` and `playwright_generated.go`, restore the
  `SourceFiles` calls, the `unreadable-source` branch and `unbounded` in
  `internal/appflows/selection.go`; no persisted store changes, and `plan.generated` is omitted
  when nothing is declared.

- **AFP-V0-445:** (proposed, experimental; accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2705, part of GitHub #1814)
  `corvint affected audit --changes FILE` MUST read one bounded `corvint-affected-audit-input/0`
  manifest (at most 1 MiB and 256 changes; each change a unique `id`, full `base` and `head`
  commit ids, `format` `junit` or `playwright-json`, `baseline` and `changed` outcome files
  relative to the manifest, each at most 32 MiB and 100,000 tests, and an optional
  repository-relative `path_prefix` joined to each test file). For each change it MUST plan `head`
  against `base` with the default `affected` selector in a private `git clone --shared` checkout
  that is removed afterwards, so the audited repository's worktree, index, refs and cache are
  untouched. A test whose status differs from the baseline, or that the baseline lacks, MUST
  count as one that had to run; it is a miss when the plan is BOUNDED and neither a selected
  test file nor a selected unit (`classname`, or `go:` plus it) names it. An UNKNOWN scope is a
  fallback that selects everything. The canonical `corvint-affected-audit/0` report MUST list,
  per change, the must-rerun tests, misses, selected and observed test counts, selected units,
  scope, fallback, unknown reasons and elapsed milliseconds, and an aggregate with the miss
  count, the exact one-sided 95% Clopper-Pearson upper bound on the per-change miss rate, and
  the rule of three when no change missed. The `e2e-compile` and `appmap-impact` selectors MUST
  be recorded `NOT_RUN`. The command MUST exit 1 on any miss, 2 on an input error
  (`unsupported-affected-audit`) or a planning error (the `affected` error code, its message
  prefixed with the change id), and 0 otherwise. A Playwright `flaky` status is its own status,
  so a change from `expected` to `flaky` is a test that had to run.
  Non-goals: running tests; the Playwright, e2e-safe and appmap selectors; per-file selection
  scoring. Failure modes: a flaky test whose status changes for reasons unrelated to the change
  is reported as a miss (the operator removes it from the outcomes); a test the changed run
  dropped is not counted; an outcome file whose `file` or `classname` does not match the plan's
  test paths or unit ids reports a miss rather than hiding one. Rollback: delete
  `cmd/corvint/affected_audit.go` and its dispatch; nothing is persisted. Amended by AFP-V0-447:
  the report is `corvint-affected-audit/1`, a planning error no longer exits 2, and a dropped
  test is no longer ignored.

- **AFP-V0-447:** (proposed, experimental; accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2705, GitHub #1814; amends AFP-V0-445)
  `corvint affected audit` MUST score every available selector against observed outcomes and
  MUST NOT turn missing observation into a safe result. (1) Changes. Each manifest change MAY
  name `kind` `replay` (the default: full `base` and `head`), `revert` (a full `revert` commit id
  reverted on `base`, with `-m 1` for a merge) or `mutant` (a `diff` patch file relative to the
  manifest, at most 8 MiB, applied with `git apply --index` on `base`); a revert or mutant MUST be
  committed in the private shared clone with a fixed identity and epoch dates so its synthesized
  `head` is the same on every run, and MUST NOT write a ref, object or file to the audited
  repository. A change that cannot be cloned, checked out, reverted or applied is an input error.
  (2) Selectors. Each change MAY add `appmap` `{map, ledger?}` (files relative to the manifest)
  and `e2e` `{manifest}` (repository-relative). The audit MUST run `affected` on every change,
  `appmap-impact` (`appmap.Impact` at the change head, full lists) on each change that names a
  map, and `e2e-compile` on each change that names a manifest, all in the private clone.
  `e2e-compile` MUST run out of process as `corvint-test e2e compile` (the companion beside the
  running executable, else on PATH) from base to head, with its scenarios written to scratch
  beside the clone, so the core binary does not link the CCE environment's `net/http`
  (invariant 7); an unavailable companion is the error `e2e-compiler-unavailable`, and a failed
  run, an unreadable report or a report with truncated scenarios is `e2e-compile-failed`; any other selector failure is `selector-error`; a selector a change does not configure is `NOT_RUN` with the
  reason, never SAFE. `affected` falls back (selects everything) when its scope is not BOUNDED,
  `appmap-impact` when its verdict is `full-suite-required`; `e2e-compile` never falls back. An
  appmap report with any omitted entry, and any selector error, MUST be recorded as scope `ERROR`
  with the error code and message and verdict UNKNOWN, not as an exit 2. A test is selected by a
  fallback, a selected test file, a selected unit (`classname`, or `go:` plus it), or an appmap
  test key `file > title` (a Playwright `[project] ` prefix is ignored). (3) Observation.
  Outcomes (`format`, `baseline`, `changed`) are all present or all absent; absent makes the
  change's observation UNKNOWN. A test whose changed status differs from the baseline, or that the
  baseline lacks, had to run. A baseline test missing from the changed run is `removed` only when
  its file was tracked at `base` and is not tracked at the change head, and otherwise
  `unobserved`. (4) Scoring. Per change and selector the `corvint-affected-audit/1` report MUST
  give the verdict (`SAFE`, `MISS`, `UNKNOWN`, `NOT_RUN`), scope (`BOUNDED`, `FALLBACK`, `ERROR`,
  `NOT_RUN`), the selector's own state, misses (each with its test identity, under the change id),
  selected tests, true positives, unobserved unselected tests, recall (true positives over tests
  that had to run) and precision (true positives over selected observed tests) to six decimals,
  `UNDEFINED` for a zero denominator and `UNKNOWN` when observation is UNKNOWN, the selector
  errored, or an unobserved test could change the ratio. MISS takes precedence over UNKNOWN, and
  UNKNOWN over SAFE. (5) Aggregate. Per selector the report MUST give changes run, observed
  (SAFE or MISS) and unknown change counts, changes with a miss, fallback changes, micro-averaged
  recall and precision (`UNKNOWN` when any run change, or that ratio for any run change, is UNKNOWN,
  or none is observed, `NOT_RUN`
  when the selector never ran), and the exact one-sided 95% Clopper-Pearson bound over the observed
  changes (`UNKNOWN` when any run change is UNKNOWN, since it could be a miss, or none is observed;
  the rule of three when none missed). (6) Exit.
  1 on any miss, else 3 when any run selector has an UNKNOWN change, else 0; 2 only on an input
  error (`unsupported-affected-audit`) or a write failure; `ok` is true only on 0.
  Non-goals: running tests; generating mutants; the Playwright-config and e2e-safe `affected`
  profiles; the appmap map build; weighting changes. Failure modes: a flaky test is reported as a
  miss unless removed from the outcomes; an outcome file whose `file` does not match a tracked path
  makes a dropped test unobserved (UNKNOWN), never removed; a revert that conflicts on `base` or a
  patch that does not apply is an input error, not a skipped change; an `e2e-compile` miss can
  mean the existing suite, not the compiler, covers the change, so its recall is a measure of the
  compiled scenarios alone. Rollback: restore the AFP-V0-445 `/0` report from Git; nothing is
  persisted, and the input profile is unchanged, so `/0` manifests stay valid.

### Two-repository e2e inputs for the selection audit (AFP-V0-490 to AFP-V0-493; GitHub #1870)

- **AFP-V0-490:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); GitHub #1870;
  amends AFP-V0-447 (2)) A change's `e2e` object MAY also name `manifest_repo` (an alias),
  `repos` (alias to absolute tests-repository root) and `repo_revisions` (alias to full commit
  id). The audit MUST pass them to `corvint-test e2e compile` exactly as its `--repo ALIAS=ROOT`,
  `--manifest-repo ALIAS` and `--repo-revision ALIAS=REV` flags (CCE-V0-130 to CCE-V0-136), in
  alias order, so an adopter whose suite and manifest live in a second repository is scored
  rather than reported `e2e-map-unavailable`. The second repository is read in place, at its
  stated revision or else its HEAD, and never written.
- **AFP-V0-491:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); GitHub #1870)
  Those inputs MUST be checked before any change runs, by the same `appmap.BindRootFlags` parse
  and `Roots.Check` the companion uses plus: every root absolute and clean
  (`rootalias.ValidRoot`), every `repos` and `repo_revisions` key a valid alias
  (`rootalias.Valid`, so a key holding `=` cannot re-split into another alias and root), every
  revision a full lowercase commit id, `manifest_repo` and every `repo_revisions` alias naming a
  `repos` alias, and no `repos` root that is the audited repository itself, directly or through a
  symlink (the compile runs on a clone, so the companion's own `--root` collision check cannot
  see it). A violation is the input error
  `unsupported-affected-audit` (exit 2), never an UNKNOWN selection.
- **AFP-V0-492:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); GitHub #1870)
  An `e2e-compile` selection MUST record, as `roots`, the aliased-root pins the compile report
  gives (alias, revision read, inputs, and `stated` for a `repo_revisions` revision), so the
  tests revision scored is in the `corvint-affected-audit/1` report; one-repository selections
  omit it. `corvint help` MUST say that the input, outcome, diff and map files must be regular
  files and that a symlink is refused.
- **AFP-V0-493:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); GitHub #1870;
  amends AFP-V0-447 (3)) When a change's `e2e` names any `repos`, a baseline test missing from
  the changed run MUST be `unobserved`, never `removed`: the suite may live in the second
  repository, read at one revision for base and head, so the audited repository's tracked files
  are no evidence of its removal, and a same-path application file deleted by the change MUST
  NOT suppress UNKNOWN.
  Non-goals: two-repository inputs for `appmap-impact`, which reads a pre-built map whose
  `roots` already pin its tests side and diffs only the audited repository; accepting a symbolic
  revision or a relative root. Failure modes: a tests root that is not a Git worktree top or
  lacks the stated commit is an `e2e-compile-failed` UNKNOWN for that change, as before; a
  `repos` entry naming the audited repository is refused by the companion. Rollback: revert the
  change; manifests without the new members are unchanged and the report gains only the
  optional `roots` member.

### Rule (d) refusals name the import chain (AFP-V0-500; V1-2823)

- **AFP-V0-500:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2823)
  When `unbounded-readers` refuses a unit that is not in `units` (AFP-V0-025), it MUST print, for
  each such unit, one line naming the unit, every edge of the chain that makes it an AFP-V0-012
  rule (d) unbounded reader, from the unit to the unbounded reader, and that reader's own
  unbounded read: `<unit>: import chain <unit> imports <next>, ..., <next> imports <reader>;
  <reader> is the unbounded reader (<reason>)`. An edge that is only a test import is printed
  `test-imports`, any other dependency edge `reaches`; a unit that is its own cause is printed
  `<unit>: its own read is unbounded (<reason>)`. `Graph.UnboundedChain` returns that chain,
  cause first, from the same walk that computes the AFP-V0-192 cause, so the chain and cause never
  disagree. Non-goals: changing which units are unbounded, the report JSON or the exit status.
  Failure modes: a unit with no recorded chain prints `<unit>: no recorded cause` and is still
  refused. Rollback: revert the change; the refusal loses only the chain lines.

### CI wall time: shard balance, the narrowing verdict and reused shards (AFP-V0-460 to AFP-V0-462; V1-1753, V1-2707, V1-2113)

- **AFP-V0-460:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-1753;
  amends the AFP-V0-422 marker) Because the AFP-V0-040 report abstains on self-hosted shards, the
  balance of those shards MUST be reported separately. (1) The AFP-V0-422 marker step of
  `go-product-shard` MUST append `packageSeconds=<s>` to `shard-<n>.self-hosted`, where `<s>` is
  the floor of the summed `Elapsed` of the package-level `pass`, `fail` and `skip` events of the
  shard's `go test -json` stream; a missing or unreadable stream leaves the bare marker, and the
  step stays best-effort. (2) When all N markers are present, `ci-shard-cost-drift` MUST run the
  advisory step `Report the self-hosted shard balance (advisory, AFP-V0-460)`: with all N sums it
  writes the slowest shard's sum, the mean, the slowest as a percentage of the mean and every sum
  to the job log and summary, and MUST emit a `::warning` naming `package-costs.json` and
  `test-slices.json` as due for a refresh when the unrounded percentage exceeds `BOUND_PERCENT` (125);
  fewer than N sums, or a zero total, reports `Not measured` and no finding. The step never fails
  the job, and never changes placement, a table or a check. (3) The tables MUST still be
  refreshed only under AFP-V0-022 and AFP-V0-060 from a hosted run: they were refreshed from hosted
  run 40122992631 (revision 16fb20b2, go1.27.3, 373 packages; `cmd/corvint` 3 slices,
  `internal/tasks/store` 4, `internal/tasks/cli` 2, each at most 350,592 ms). Non-goals: a box cost
  table, which AFP-V0-060 (1) and (2), AFP-V0-164 and the AFP-V0-422 falsifier exclude; an
  automatic refresh. Failure modes: box shards measure longer and more unevenly than hosted
  timings predict, so while the box runs the shards the warning can fire on a freshly refreshed
  table (box runs 40216255651 and 40209710856 measured 168 and 150 percent of the mean; the replay predicts 172 and 144 with the refreshed tables); the
  remedy then is an owner amendment that admits a box-calibrated table, not a hosted refresh.
  Falsifier: a balance step that fails the job, writes a table, or warns at or below the bound.
  Rollback: revert the `packageSeconds` lines, the `boxed` output and the balance step.
- **AFP-V0-461:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2707)
  (1) The `Go tests (full until matching shadow qualification)` step of `go-product-shard` MUST
  set `PR_HEAD` to the pull request's head commit, because `.github/forward-rows/outcomes.sh`
  records it in every shard's outcomes; without it, every pull-request forward row is `INCOMPLETE`,
  and the AFP-V0-098 kill switch trips. (2) `.github/forward-rows/accept.sh` MUST print the
  AFP-V0-097 verdict and its reason to the job log as the line
  `AFP-V0-097 narrowing verdict: <verdict> (<reason>)` and as a `::notice`, and write the same line
  to the step summary when one exists, so that a declined narrowing is visible in the log of every
  run. Non-goals: no change to the forward-bar window, its thresholds or the kill switch; the rows
  recorded without a head stay `INCOMPLETE` until fresh rows fill the window. Failure mode:
  `accept.sh` runs from the base, so (2) takes effect on the first run after this change is on
  `main`. Falsifier: a pull-request outcome without the head commit, or a verdict that reaches
  only the step summary. Rollback: revert the `PR_HEAD` line and the three output lines.
- **AFP-V0-462:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2113;
  amends the AFP-V0-420 routing) When `docs-plan` succeeded with mode `DOCS` or `REUSE`,
  `go-product-shard` MUST run on `ubuntu-24.04`, even where AFP-V0-420 would route it to the box.
  In both modes, the `Go tests (full until matching shadow qualification)` step does not run on
  either runner, so the route changes only where the shard waits, not what it verifies. Every
  other case keeps the AFP-V0-420 expression. The REUSE decision itself stays the AFP-V0-024,
  AFP-V0-046 and AFP-V0-170 exact-tree binding, and artifact-integrity's reuse stays its own
  exact-tree record; this requirement trusts no artifact by its name. Non-goals: no new reuse
  path; no change to the REUSE or DOCS decision. Failure mode: a hosted shard spends its
  provisioning time (about a minute) instead of the box's queue wait. Before the change, the REUSE
  shards of merge-queue runs 40250398874 and 40209861746 waited 17 and 25 minutes for a box runner,
  for about 25 seconds of work each. Falsifier: a DOCS or REUSE shard on a self-hosted runner, or a
  FULL shard moved off the AFP-V0-420 route. Rollback: drop the docs-plan clause from `runs-on`.
- **AFP-V0-463:** (accepted by the owner, owner chat 2026-10-26; V1-2707; amends the AFP-V0-461
  non-goal for the rows recorded without a head) `bar.jq` MUST count as `INFRA`, not `INCOMPLETE`,
  every `INCOMPLETE` forward row whose `pr<N>-<head sha>` key is listed in
  `.github/forward-rows/infra-reclassified.txt`, and `bar.sh`, `verdict.sh` and `accept.sh` MUST pass
  that file as `--rawfile reclassified`. The list holds the 92 epoch-1 keys whose artifacts were
  each read and whose only recorded reason was `outcomes shard N is pull_request of head null`, the
  collector defect fixed by AFP-V0-461 (1). Those rows measure the collector, not the shadow
  selector, which is the AFP-V0-052 meaning of `INFRA`. Measured on 2026-10-26 over all 694
  forward-row names: the kill-switch window falls from 7 to 3 `INCOMPLETE` rows; the epoch bar
  stays MET (557 complete, 69 failing, 0 MISS). Non-goals: no change to the thresholds, the
  window size, the epoch, or any class other than `INCOMPLETE`; the other 21 `INCOMPLETE` rows
  (missing shadow artifacts, partial shard sets) stay `INCOMPLETE`; no automatic listing. A key
  is added only with a recorded owner decision. Failure mode: a listed key whose newest row is
  `CLEAN` or `MISS` is unaffected, because only `INCOMPLETE` is rewritten; a missing file fails
  the jq invocation, which every caller already treats as unclassified (FULL). Falsifier: a listed
  `MISS` row counted as `INFRA`, an unlisted `INCOMPLETE` row counted as `INFRA`, or a caller that
  omits the file. Rollback: delete the file and the three `--rawfile` arguments.

### Fixture-sited frontiers (AFP-V0-446, V1-2708)

- **AFP-V0-446:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2708)
  A located, unpinned TypeScript `typescript:relative-import-unresolved` site whose file a unit
  under a `testdata` directory owns, and whose `./` or `../` specifier, resolved against that file
  with any query or fragment dropped, names a path inside the outermost `testdata` directory,
  SHALL be that unit's own frontier (`Unit.Frontier`, V1-0289), not the plugin's, when (i) a Go
  unit lies in that directory's parent or an ancestor of it, so the Go testdata convention
  (AFP-V0-004, rule (b)) already traces every change below the directory to the package, and
  (ii) no import edge crosses the `testdata` boundary anywhere in the unit's import-connected
  component: neither a unit outside importing one inside nor a unit inside importing one outside.
  Such a site names a file absent from the fixture, so no dirty path outside the fixture is what
  it loads. The site then raises `LANGUAGE_FRONTIER` only in a plan whose reach includes the unit,
  such as a change to the fixture file or to a fixture it imports, and counts once in
  `frontierSites`. Every other site stays plugin-wide: a relative specifier whose path leaves the
  `testdata` directory, a bare or aliased specifier, a computed import (`typescript:dynamic-import`)
  and every other frontier reason, whose target nothing bounds. Without (i) the graph MUST raise
  the unit's frontier to its plugin again, so it bears on every plan the plugin takes part in, as
  before; a promoted site is listed in full, outside the plugin's per-reason `+N` bound
  (TJAA-V0-052), which applies to the sites the plugin names itself. Without (ii) the plugin MUST
  keep every site of that component plugin-wide. A site no unit owns, a pinned site (TJAA-V0-058),
  a reason raised without a site and the Playwright profile, which reads the site set before this
  placement, are unchanged.
  Failure modes: rule (ii) keeps every observed fixture wired to repository code plugin-wide
  (`internal/jstestprovider/testdata/route-stubs` and `internal/doccorpus/testdata/behavior-events`
  import a server stub beside their package); a localized site whose specifier would later resolve
  because a file appears inside the fixture is a change below `testdata`, which rule (b) traces
  to the host package and the unit's own reach to the fixture. A `testdata` directory holding a
  `go.mod` is still the Go plugin's nested-module frontier (AFP-V0-191). Falsifier: a plan over an
  unrelated TypeScript source `BOUNDED` while a fixture under a Go package's `testdata` carries a
  computed import, a specifier leaving the directory or an aliased one; a plan over such a source
  naming an in-fixture site of a component with no crossing import edge; or a plan over that
  fixture file omitting the site. Rollback: drop the `localizeFixtureFrontier` call in
  `unitsSource` and `promoteUnhostedFixtureFrontier` in `build`; unit frontiers are part of the
  canonical graph, so digests and caches rebuild either way.

### Selector reach shared with the planner (AFP-V0-464, V1-2727)

- **AFP-V0-464:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2727)
  Amending AFP-V0-012, the static selector `internal/gateselect` SHALL reach a package's
  dependents as the planner reaches them (`Graph.traverse` then `Graph.testUsersOf` in
  `internal/liveverify/affected`): the reverse closure over edges taken from non-test files, then
  one level of packages whose `_test.go` files alone import or name a reached package, the seeds
  included, never expanded past that level. A test file is compiled only into its own package's
  test binary, so no importer of that package links what the test imports. For rules (a) and (b)
  the non-test edges are imports and path tokens whose component run names a package directory,
  and the root-module literal held by a non-test file; a root-module literal held only by test
  files is a test-only edge. For rule (d) the closure from each root-locating package SHALL follow
  import edges only, as `Graph.unboundedReaders` does: a path token naming the locator's directory
  links none of its code into the naming package. Rules (c) and the AFP-V0-023 declarations are
  unchanged, and a package whose test files name a dirty path is still a rule (c) reader.
  Failure modes: a package that reaches a root locator only through code a test runs as a
  subprocess (`go build ./cmd/x`, then executing it) is no longer a rule (d) dependent of it; such
  a package runs `go` or a built binary, which the planner's own Go scan reports as its own
  unbounded read, and `make unbounded-readers-check` holds the reviewed set
  (`.corvint/unbounded-readers.json`, AFP-V0-025) equal to the planner's. A plan that cannot be
  produced is still the full run. Falsifier: a fixture with a test-only import or a path-token
  holder where the selector's rule (a) or rule (d) reach differs from the planner's
  `ReachedUnitIDs` or `UnboundedReaders`, or a package whose test binary links a dirty package or a
  root locator through non-test imports and that the selector does not reach. Rollback: restore
  `importers` as the one edge list `link` fills from every file and `unresolved` walking
  `dependents`.

### Spec directory declared as documentation (AFP-V0-465, V1-2708, V1-2726)

- **AFP-V0-465:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2708,
  V1-2726) Amending AFP-V0-160 (6), this repository SHALL declare `docs/specs/` as documentation
  in `.corvint/documentation-paths.json` beside `docs/build-log/`. A spec, its generated
  `INDEX.json`, `README.md` and `REQUIREMENTS.tsv` are read only by the units whose path literals,
  declared read scopes or unbounded reads reach them, which AFP-V0-160 (3) still selects, and by
  repository gates outside any unit; no plugin claims them as source. So a change whose only
  unowned dirty paths are specs is no `UNOWNED_DIRTY_PATH` and takes no plugin into the plan
  (AFP-V0-160 (4)): it brings no plugin-wide `LANGUAGE_FRONTIER` from a language none of whose
  units reads the spec. The selection and the `corvint test` run set are unchanged; the
  `participants` rule is unchanged, so a dirty path no plugin claims and no declaration covers
  still takes every plugin in. Failure modes: a spec that a test reads through a computed path
  with no literal, no declared scope and no unbounded read is not seen, the AFP-V0-160 blind spot,
  and a file under `docs/specs/` that becomes a build input (an embed, a generated source) is a
  declaration error the owner corrects; a path a plugin claims stays `UNINDEXED_SOURCE_PATH`.
  Falsifier: a plan for a change under `docs/specs/` whose selection or `corvint test --dry-run`
  invocations differ from the same change without the declaration, or a unit that reads a spec
  without a literal, scope or unbounded read reaching it. Rollback: remove `docs/specs/` from the
  declaration and from `TestRepositoryDeclaresBuildLogDocumentation_AFPV0160`.
### Advisory pytest command (AFP-V0-472 to AFP-V0-477, V1-2760)

The Python adapter selects test units, but until this slice `advice.checks` named no command for
them, so an agent on a Python repository got a plan it could not run. These requirements add one
advisory pytest entry beside the Go (AFP-V0-009) and RSpec (AFP-V0-066) entries, from the plan and
a bounded read of the configuration files pytest itself would read. They amend AFP-V0-009's
advice list only; `provider`, the plan, the compact form and `corvint test` are unchanged.

- **AFP-V0-472:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2760)
  Command and working directory. When `plan.selected` holds a unit whose id begins `python:` with
  a `tests` file ending `.py`, and AFP-V0-473 to AFP-V0-476 admit it, `advice.checks` MUST gain one
  `advisory` entry with source `affected-plan`, placed after the Go and RSpec entries, whose
  command is `python -m pytest` followed by the sorted, deduplicated test modules AFP-V0-474 keeps,
  each prefixed `./` and POSIX single-quoted as AFP-V0-066 quotes spec files. The command runs from
  the repository root, the directory every advice command assumes; `python -m` puts that directory
  on `sys.path`, which is what a flat-layout project needs. The reason counts the Python test units
  and names the repository root, the configuration file pytest reads (or that pytest's defaults
  apply) and any AFP-V0-475 source root.
- **AFP-V0-473:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2760)
  Declared use of pytest. The entry exists only when the repository declares pytest: the
  AFP-V0-474 configuration file holds a pytest section (a `pyproject.toml` pytest only falls back
  to, without a pytest table, declares nothing); a selected file, or a unit of the graph, is a
  `conftest.py`; or a root `pyproject.toml`, `setup.cfg`, `setup.py`, `tox.ini`, `Pipfile` or
  `requirements*.txt`, each read only as a regular file of at most 256 KiB, names `pytest` as a
  whole word. Otherwise `unknown` gains
  `NO_ADVISORY_PYTEST_COMMAND: the plan selected Python test units but the repository declares no pytest configuration, conftest.py or pytest dependency`.
  The dependency search is bounded: at most 4096 repository-root entries listed, 16
  `requirements*.txt` files and 1 MiB of manifest bytes read in all. Reaching a bound before
  `pytest` is found yields
  `NO_ADVISORY_PYTEST_COMMAND: no pytest configuration or conftest.py is declared and the root dependency manifests exceed the discovery bound (<bound>)`
  instead, naming the bound that was hit.
- **AFP-V0-474:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2760)
  Configuration discovery and `python_files`. The configuration is the one pytest 8's
  `determine_setup` selects for the test modules as its arguments, run from the repository root:
  searching from their deepest common directory up to the repository root, the first of
  `pytest.ini` (always, even when empty), `.pytest.ini` with a `[pytest]` section,
  `pyproject.toml` with a `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` or `[tool.pytest]` table, `tox.ini` with a
  `[pytest]` section and `setup.cfg` with a `[tool:pytest]` section, in that order per directory;
  when none holds one, the first `pyproject.toml` seen is the configuration file with pytest's
  defaults. When that search finds nothing, a `setup.py` regular file between the common directory
  and the repository root stops discovery with pytest's defaults; otherwise the same search runs
  from each module's own directory in argument order. Values are read as pytest reads them: an ini
  value is the raw text after `=` or `:`, continuation lines joined, whole-line `#` and `;`
  comments skipped, and split with POSIX shell rules (Python `shlex.split`), a value that cannot
  be split yielding
  `NO_ADVISORY_PYTEST_COMMAND: <path> sets <key> to a value pytest cannot split`; a TOML array is
  taken as its strings and a TOML string is shell-split, with `#` comments outside strings
  dropped. Its `python_files`
  patterns (default `test_*.py *_test.py`, and a present but empty value matching nothing) keep
  the candidates pytest treats as test modules, by
  Python `fnmatch` on the base name, or on the path end for a pattern with `/`; the plugin's other
  test-directory files, such as `conftest.py`, helpers and `__init__.py`, drop out. Because
  filtering can deepen the common directory, the search repeats over the kept modules until the
  configuration file is the same, at most three times; otherwise `unknown` gains
  `NO_ADVISORY_PYTEST_COMMAND: the pytest configuration pytest would read for the selected test files does not settle (<a>, then <b>)`.
  A candidate configuration file that exists but is not a regular file of at most 256 KiB yields
  `NO_ADVISORY_PYTEST_COMMAND: <path> may hold the pytest configuration but is not a readable regular file of at most 256 KiB`.
  No `rootdir`, `-c`, `--rootdir`, `PYTEST_ADDOPTS` or `addopts` value is evaluated; pytest
  applies the file's own `addopts`, `testpaths` and plugins when the command runs.
- **AFP-V0-475:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2760)
  Source-root module path. The command is prefixed `PYTHONPATH='<root>[<sep><root>]' ` naming each
  Python source root (`src`, `lib`, `python`, the adapter's `SourceRoots`) that is not itself a
  regular package (no `<root>/__init__.py`), that the configuration's `pythonpath` (resolved
  against the configuration file's directory) does not already name, and that an emitted module,
  inside the root or outside it, reaches through the graph's import edges to a `python:<root>.`
  unit. Such a test imports the root's modules by their root-relative names, so it fails to import
  without the root on the module path, as a src-layout project's tests do; pytest puts a test's
  own directory there, not the root. `<sep>` is the path-list separator of the platform Corvint
  runs on (`:`, or `;` on Windows), where the agent runs the advice; the env-prefix form itself
  still assumes a POSIX shell, as every advice command does.
- **AFP-V0-476:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2760)
  Named reasons. A selection whose modules all fail `python_files` yields no entry and
  `NO_ADVISORY_PYTEST_COMMAND: the plan selected <n> Python test unit(s) but no selected file matches python_files <patterns>`
  (`<patterns>` is `(empty)` for an empty value);
  more than 1024 modules yield
  `NO_ADVISORY_PYTEST_COMMAND: <n> selected test files exceed the 1024-file bound`. Every reason
  this slice cannot name a runnable command is exactly one such line, so a Python selection never
  ends with neither a command nor a reason.
- **AFP-V0-477:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2760)
  Scope. A plan that selects no `python:` unit with a `.py` test file gains nothing, so every
  receipt without one keeps its exact bytes; the Go and RSpec entries, their unknown lines, the
  AFP-V0-038 compact form and `provider` (which keeps only `go`) are unchanged, and the pytest
  command is never compacted.

Non-goals: no execution, interpreter, virtual environment or dependency resolution; no `tox`,
`nox`, `ansible-test`, `unittest` or Django runner; no node ids or `-k` expressions (whole modules
only); no pytest plugin, `conftest.py` hook or `pytest_collect_file` model; no `PYTHONPATH` beyond
the adapter's source roots; and no change to Python selection itself.
Failure modes: the command can still fail where the environment lacks the project's dependencies
or Python version, or where a project's own runner owns its tests (ansible `test/integration`
targets reached by the Python adapter are listed as modules and may not collect under plain
pytest); the selection precision is the adapter's (AFP-V0-181 to AFP-V0-186), and the unknown
frontier and mandatory checks still apply (AFP-V0-004). A `[tool.pytest.ini_options]` table
declared with dotted keys or an inline table is not read, so its `python_files` fall back to the
default. Falsifier: a Python plan with no pytest entry and no `NO_ADVISORY_PYTEST_COMMAND` line; an
entry whose modules pytest's own configuration would not treat as test modules; a src-layout
command without the `PYTHONPATH` prefix that its tests need; or a non-Python receipt whose bytes
change. Rollback: drop the `advisoryPytestCheck` call in `compileAffectedAdvice`; no state, cache
or wire member besides the advice entries depends on it.
### Frame-metadata calls and untested-package compile checks (AFP-V0-466 to AFP-V0-469; V1-2758, V1-2759)

- **AFP-V0-466:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2758)
  Superseded by AFP-V0-810: the earlier metadata-only import exception did not distinguish
  file contents from frame metadata and still selected every navidrome logger importer.
- **AFP-V0-467:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2758)
  `runtime.Callers`, `runtime.FuncForPC` and `runtime.CallersFrames` SHALL stay outside rule (d),
  as before this change and as in the gate tool: rule (d) matches the exact callee name.
  Since AFP-V0-810, `internal/gateselect` (which `tools/gate-affected-select` runs) excludes
  `runtime.Caller` too, so the two rule (d) sets stay equal (AFP-V0-464). Residual:
  `Func.FileLine` and `Frame.File` also yield a source file name. Matching them was measured at
  base 4c6673d6c and widened teleport's selections from 32/58 to 50/58 (p01), from 59/218 to
  167/218 Go units (p08) and from 15/69 to 59/69 (p10), through `lib/utils` logging helpers that
  read function names. That is left for its own decision.
- **AFP-V0-468:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2759)
  When the plan reaches Go units without tests through a `DIRECT_SOURCE_CHANGE` or
  `DEPENDENCY_PATH` witness, `advice.checks` SHALL gain one advisory entry,
  `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go test -count=1 -run '^$' <packages>`, over those units' import paths, sorted
  and shell-quoted like the AFP-V0-009 command, with source `affected-plan`: it compiles and vets
  each changed untested package and its in-module untested dependents and runs no test. A unit
  reached only as an `UNBOUNDED_READER` is not listed. An `EMPTY_SELECTION` covered by this entry
  or by an AFP-V0-469 entry adds no `NO_ADVISORY_GO_COMMAND` line; `NO_SELECTABLE_TEST` and
  `UNOWNED_DIRTY_PATH` stay in `plan.unknown`, because compiling is not running behaviour. Past the
  AFP-V0-009 command bound the entry is replaced by
  `NO_ADVISORY_GO_COMPILE_COMMAND: <n> untested Go packages exceed the <bound>-pattern bound`.
  The plan and `provider.go` wire are unchanged.
- **AFP-V0-469:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2759)
  For each directory holding a dirty `go.mod` or `go.sum`, `advice.checks` SHALL gain one
  advisory entry, `GOTOOLCHAIN=local go vet ./...` at the root or
  `(cd '<dir>' && GOTOOLCHAIN=local go vet ./...)` for a nested module, ordered after the
  AFP-V0-468 entry and by directory. The governing `go.work` is the nearest one at or above the
  directory inside the repository, the one the go tool uses. When one exists and its `use`
  directives do not list the directory, the command is prefixed `GOWORK=off`, because the go tool
  refuses `./...` in a module the workspace does not use. The prefix is the same POSIX
  environment-assignment form as the `GOTOOLCHAIN=local` prefix every advisory command already
  carries. A governing `go.work` that cannot be read within the manifest bound or parsed adds
  `NO_ADVISORY_GO_MODULE_COMMAND: <paths> changed but its governing <go.work> is <reason>`
  instead. It type-checks every package and test of the module under
  its new requirements and runs nothing; the reason says the module's tests still carry the
  behaviour. A directory under `testdata` or `vendor`, or with a `_` or `.` prefixed component,
  is one the go tool never builds and adds nothing, unless the governing `go.work`, itself outside
  such a directory, lists it in a `use` directive: explicit membership is checked first. A dirty `go.mod` or `go.sum` whose directory's
  `go.mod` declares no readable module adds
  `NO_ADVISORY_GO_MODULE_COMMAND: <paths> changed but <dir>/go.mod declares no readable module`,
  the dirty paths of that directory joined by `, `.
  `plan.scope` stays `UNKNOWN` for the unowned path.
  Failure modes: frame metadata alone does not identify a file reader (AFP-V0-810).
  Computed reads or directory changes derived from frame values need separate read evidence;
  this static selector does not infer interprocedural data flow. A `go.work` above the
  repository root, or one named by `GOWORK`, is not seen. The compile entry proves only that
  code builds. Rollback: revert the change; no wire format changes.

### Shadow selector tiers and cost (AFP-V0-480 to AFP-V0-484)

These requirements measure, on real pull-request traffic, what each candidate selector would cost
and miss next to the shadow selection S: B (changed packages only), C (B plus one hop of
importers, test imports included) and D (B plus two hops). They are measurements only. Nothing in
them selects, executes or narrows a test, and none of them feeds the AFP-V0-029 status, the
AFP-V0-030 kill switch, `accept.sh`, or `CORVINT_PR_NARROWING`.

- **AFP-V0-480:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); owner lane
  request 2026-10-26; no ticket; effective only after the owner's `ci-control-plane` review of the
  `.github` change) The shadow step of `go-product-shard` MUST run
  `.github/forward-rows/tiers.sh "$PR_BASE" "$TESTED_MERGE" "$d/tiers.json"` in the tested checkout.
  `selection.jq` MUST then carry that document in `corvint-pr-selection/0` as two optional additive
  fields: `tiers` (`changed`, `hop1`, `hop2`, `full`, `universe`, `millis`) and `tierIncomplete`.
  A selection document without them stays valid. The tiers are defined as follows:
  - `changed` (B) holds the packages whose directory holds a `.go` file that
    `git diff --name-only --no-renames BASE TARGET` names.
  - `hop1` (C) is B plus every package whose `Imports`, `TestImports` or `XTestImports` name a
    member of B.
  - `hop2` (D) is C plus every package whose imports, under the same rule, name a member of C.

  The metadata comes only from `go list -e -json=ImportPath,Imports,TestImports,XTestImports,EmbedFiles,TestEmbedFiles,XTestEmbedFiles,EmbedPatterns,TestEmbedPatterns,XTestEmbedPatterns ./...`
  and `go list -m`, run with `GOPROXY=off` and an empty `GOFLAGS`. There is no network, no build
  and no test.

  Any of the following changed paths makes every tier the string `FULL`, never an empty list, and
  `full` names up to 20 reasons:
  - a path under `.github/`;
  - a `go.mod`, `go.sum`, `go.work` or `go.work.sum`;
  - a path with a `testdata` segment;
  - a file that a package embeds;
  - a deleted file (`git diff --diff-filter=D`) in or under the directory of a package with any
    embed pattern, since the target's `go list` cannot name a deleted file as embedded;
  - any other non-Go file, except a path that AFP-V0-520 exempts;
  - a `.go` file outside every listed package.

  A failure of `git`, `go list` or `jq` writes `tiers: null`, with the reason in
  `tierIncomplete`, and the step continues. `millis` is the script's wall time, the first 13
  digits of `date +%s%N`, or null where `date` has no `%N`.
- **AFP-V0-481:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11), as AFP-V0-480) `collect.sh` MUST pass the trusted checkout's
  `.github/cishards/package-costs.json` to `classify.sh` as `ROW_COSTS`, and the forward-rows sparse
  checkout MUST include `.github/cishards`. `classify.sh` MUST hand the file to `row.jq` only when
  it parses as a JSON object. `row.jq` MUST add the additive row field `shadowCost`, built as
  follows:
  - Full is every package with an outcome in any shard of the outcomes run.
  - Each selector is intersected with Full and priced by summing its milliseconds. The selectors
    are S (the union of the shards' `selected`) and the tiers B, C and D. A `FULL` tier is Full.
  - A package that the file does not price takes the file's median, the value at index
    `floor(len/2)` of the sorted milliseconds (the `.github/cishards` convention). `unpriced`
    counts Full's unpriced packages.
  - `permille` is `round(1000 * ms / Full ms)`.

  A cost file that is missing, or that is not `corvint-ci-package-costs/0` with only positive
  milliseconds, prices nothing (`ms` and `permille` null), and `shadowCost.incomplete` says so.
  The tiers are unmeasured (null) when any of the following holds, and `shadowCost.incomplete`
  names the reason:
  - no selection document carries them;
  - a selection document reports `tierIncomplete`;
  - the shards disagree;
  - the tiers fail the shape check.

  `shadowCost` MUST be bounded on its own: at most 20 `fullReasons` and 20 tier reasons, each cut
  to 300 characters. The row is first bounded to 64 KiB without `shadowCost`. The cost then takes
  the room left: its `missedPackages` are shortened first, then it is replaced by an `incomplete`
  note, and then it is null. Advisory cost data never truncates, drops or reclassifies a row.
- **AFP-V0-482:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11), as AFP-V0-480) A selector's `missedPackages` MUST be the packages that
  failed in the outcomes run (the AFP-V0-029 failing set) and lie outside that selector, less every
  FLAKY package. A FLAKY package is one whose every failing shard reran it and saw it pass, and
  which the pull-request run did not narrow out. `missed` is their count. For S, this is the row's
  `missed` less its `flaky`. A `FULL` tier never misses. `shadowCost` MUST NOT change the row's
  class, its counts or any other existing row field.
- **AFP-V0-483:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11), as AFP-V0-480) For a `CLEAN`, `FLAKY` or `MISS` row, `collect.sh` MUST
  write the step output `cost=ci-forward-cost-pr<N>-<sha40>-e<E>-<class>-f<f>-s<S>-b<B>-c<C>-d<D>-m<flags>`,
  where `<class>` and `<f>` repeat the row name's class and failing flag.
  Each slot is a four-digit permille share of Full, or `na`. The flags are four characters in the
  order S, B, C, D: `1` (missed), `0` (no miss) or `n` (not measured). The forward-rows job MUST
  upload the same `row.json` under that name in a `continue-on-error` step. The name never matches
  the AFP-V0-047 row pattern. An `INCOMPLETE`, `INFRA` or `NARROWED` row names no cost.
- **AFP-V0-484:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11), as AFP-V0-480) After it decides the status and the kill switch,
  `bar.jq` MUST add `shadowCost` (`advisory`, `epoch`, `window`) from the `ci-forward-cost-*` names
  of the epoch. A cost name prices a row only when it has the row's pull request, commit, class
  and failing flag and is listed before the row (it was uploaded after the row by the job that
  uploaded it). Of those names the one listed nearest the row wins. A row with no such name is
  unpriced, so an older run's cost never prices a re-run's row. For the
  deduplicated epoch rows, and separately for the AFP-V0-030 window rows, it reports:
  - `complete`: the `CLEAN`, `FLAKY` and `MISS` rows;
  - `priced`: those rows that have a cost name;
  - for each selector: `measured`, `medianShare`, `meanShare` (shares of Full), `missMeasured`
    and `missRows`.

  `bar.sh` MUST print one `Shadow cost, <scope>` line per scope. `verdict.sh` MUST copy the field
  to `afp-shadow-bar-verdict/0` as `shadowCost`. It MUST NOT feed the status, the kill switch,
  `accept.sh`, `CORVINT_PR_NARROWING` or any narrowing.

Non-goals: none of these requirements selects tests by tier, changes the selector, the bar
thresholds, the window, the epoch or the kill switch, or downloads row artifacts in the bar.

Failure modes:
- Most real pull requests also change non-Go files (CEMs, specs, `.taskman/`), so their tiers are
  `FULL`. That is honest, but it leaves fewer rows that measure B, C and D. AFP-V0-520 exempts the
  sealed CEMs and declared documentation; any other non-Go path still does.
- The D hop also follows test imports, which over-includes some packages.
- One cost artifact per complete row moves the AFP-V0-097 page bound slightly closer.
- The tier work runs in each of the N shards, about 0.8 s each. This was measured at 0.76 to
  0.9 s on macOS with a warm cache over 378 packages; the hosted time is `NOT_OBSERVED`.

Falsifier: any of the following refutes these requirements:
- a tier that is empty for a change that the FULL rules name;
- a `shadowCost` that changes a row's class or the bar status;
- a selection document without `tiers` that fails classification;
- a cost name for an `INCOMPLETE` row.

Rollback: revert the `tiers.sh` lines and the `--slurpfile tiers` argument in `ci.yml`, the cost
upload step and the `.github/cishards` sparse path, the `ROW_COSTS` plumbing, the `$cost` block of
`row.jq`, and the `shadowCost` blocks of `bar.jq` and `verdict.sh`. The old rows and the verdicts
stay valid, since every field is additive.

### Tier exemption for sealed CEMs and declared documentation (AFP-V0-520, V1-2763)

Intent (V1-2763 AC3; owner chat, 2026-10-27: "exempt .corvint/changes/*.cem.json and declared
documentation from the tier-FULL rule"): every batch pull request carries a sealed CEM and
documentation, so under the AFP-V0-480 non-Go rule every `ci-forward-cost` row priced B, C and D
at `1000` and the shadow comparison measured nothing.

- **AFP-V0-520:** (accepted by the owner, owner chat 2026-10-27; V1-2763; amends the AFP-V0-480
  non-Go rule; the owner's `ci-control-plane` OK covers the `.github` change) In `.github/forward-rows/tiers.jq`, a changed
  path that would raise only the AFP-V0-480 "any other non-Go file" reason MUST raise no reason when
  either holds:
  1. it is a sealed CEM under the AFP-V0-152 rule: it lies directly under `.corvint/changes/`, with
     no further `/`, and its name ends in `.cem.json` after a non-empty stem;
  2. the repository's AFP-V0-160 declaration `.corvint/documentation-paths.json` names it: it
     equals an entry, or lies under an entry that ends in `/`.

  `tiers.sh` MUST pass the declaration's bytes at the target commit to `tiers.jq` as
  `--rawfile documentation`, or an empty file when the target has none. `tiers.jq` MUST use the
  declaration only when it meets every AFP-V0-160 (1) rule: at most 64 KiB; the closed object with
  exactly `profile` `corvint-documentation-paths/0` and `paths`; at most 256 entries; each entry a
  string under the AFP-V0-023 entry grammar, never `.git`, `.corvint` or a path below either; and
  strictly ascending. Otherwise, or without the argument, it declares nothing.

  Every earlier AFP-V0-480 rule still applies first, so a `.github/` path, a module file, a
  `testdata` path, an embedded file, or a deleted file under an embedding package stays `FULL` even
  when the declaration names it. A `.go` file is never exempted. Every other non-Go path still makes
  every tier `FULL`: the CEM sidecar `.corvint/change.cem.json`, a CEM in a subdirectory of
  `.corvint/changes/` or elsewhere, `.corvint/documentation-paths.json` itself, `.taskman/`, a
  `Makefile` or a script. A change of only exempt paths has empty B, C and D lists.

Non-goals: no B, B+R+U or other new tier; no change to the AFP-V0-029 status, the AFP-V0-030 kill
switch, `accept.sh`, `CORVINT_PR_NARROWING`, the selector S, any CI job or test set, or the
affected planner; no new documentation list. The tiers stay a measurement only.

Failure modes:
- B, C and D are Go-import tiers, so they hold none of the path-literal readers of a CEM or a
  spec. A test that reads an exempt file then fails outside every tier and is reported as a miss of
  B, C and D, which is the recall that V1-2763 AC3 measures, not a hidden coverage loss.
- A declaration that `tiers.jq` reads as valid but `affected` refuses (for example, one with a
  duplicate key, which `jq` reads as its last value) still exempts its entries for the tiers.
- The declaration comes from the target commit. Changing it is itself a non-Go change that makes
  that pull request `FULL`, so it cannot exempt paths in the change that adds it.

Falsifier: any of the following refutes AFP-V0-520:
- a sealed CEM or declared path alone that makes a tier `FULL`;
- a change of an exempt path and a Go file whose tiers differ from the Go file's alone;
- a `Makefile`, `.sh`, `.corvint/change.cem.json`, `.corvint/changes/<dir>/<x>.cem.json`, or
  declared `testdata`, embedded or `.github/` path that leaves any tier a list;
- a declaration AFP-V0-160 (1) refuses that exempts a path.

Rollback: delete `sealedCEM`, `validEntry`, `documentation`, `declared` and the exemption branch
from `tiers.jq`, and the declaration read and `--rawfile documentation` argument from `tiers.sh`.
The tier documents keep the same shape, so old and new rows stay valid.

### Symlink hint and test-less readers (AFP-V0-494, AFP-V0-495; GitHub #1751)

Intent (GitHub #1751 residuals, V1-2794 and V1-2666): a `node_modules` symbolic link into another
checkout stayed `UNOWNED_DIRTY_PATH` although `.gitignore` held `node_modules/`, and the operator
was given no reason; and a test-less TypeScript helper that read a declared documentation path was
selected as a reader with no check, while the tests that import it were not selected.

- **AFP-V0-494:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2794, GitHub #1751)
  An `UNOWNED_DIRTY_PATH` unknown whose path in the observed checkout is a symbolic link MUST
  carry an operator `hint` saying that a Git ignore pattern ending in `/` matches only a
  directory, never a symlink, and that dropping the trailing `/` ignores the link. There are four
  parts.
  - (1) The planner reads only `Lstat` of that one path under the graph's disk root and never
    follows the link.
  - (2) `hint` is an additive, omitted-when-empty member of a `plan.unknown` entry; `reason` and
    the bare-path `detail` are unchanged, so a consumer keyed on `detail` is unaffected.
  - (3) The hint changes neither selection nor scope: the path stays unowned and the plan stays
    `UNKNOWN`.
  - (4) The Playwright plan's graph-frontier detail carries the hint after the bare path.
  Non-goals: rewriting the repository's ignore file; a hint for a graph built from a file system
  with no disk root. Failure modes: a failed `Lstat`, an invalid path or no disk root gives no
  hint, never a changed plan. Rollback: drop `symlinkHints`, the `Hint` field and `Graph.root`.
- **AFP-V0-495:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2666, GitHub #1751)
  Amending `AFP-V0-021`'s rule that a path-literal or path-glob reader is not traversed to its
  dependents, a selected reader that holds no test MUST be traversed when the dirty path it names
  is owned by no unit. There are three parts.
  - (1) The reach step runs the reader rule once more over only the unowned dirty paths, starts
    from the test-less readers it finds, and adds the units that depend on them and the tests
    that use those, as for a changed unit, each with a `DEPENDENCY_PATH` witness whose chain
    starts at the reader.
  - (2) A reader of an owned dirty path is still not traversed: that path's owning unit already
    carries the change, and a runner configuration that names a test path would otherwise select
    every test that imports it.
  - (3) The extra pass runs only when some selected reader holds no test and some dirty path is
    unowned.
  Non-goals: traversing a reader that holds tests, which already contributes its own checks;
  traversing readers of owned paths. Failure modes: the extra selection only widens a plan; a
  reader the rule cannot see stays unseen, as for `AFP-V0-088`. Rollback: drop
  `testlessReaderUsers`; the plan then selects the helper with no check, as before.

- **AFP-V0-496:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2872)
  The advice of `corvint affected --base SHA` MUST NOT present an exhaustive gate as the default
  next step. There are three parts.
  - (1) When the plan has a base and dirty paths, the first advisory check is
    `corvint test --base SHA --dry-run`, which prints the selected focused test argv and runs
    nothing; the per-unit Go test command follows it.
  - (2) A declared mandatory check (`make gate`, a `go test ./...` Verify line) keeps its kind and
    stays in the list, but its reason says it is required at the project terminal boundary, not as
    the default next step.
  - (3) Without a base or without dirty paths no focused command is added.
  Non-goals: executing, removing or re-kinding a declared check. Failure modes: the focused
  command is only advice and may be unusable where `corvint test` cannot plan. Rollback: drop
  `withFocusedNextStep` and restore the previous reasons.

### Lexicon tags and backslashes, `--path` recovery and the widened documentation declaration (AFP-V0-505 to AFP-V0-508; V1-2553, V1-2708, V1-2726, V1-2818)

- **AFP-V0-505:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2553, V1-2708)
  Amending the AFP-V0-021 lexicon in the Go plugin: a `/` that directly follows `<` inside a
  string literal closes a markup tag (`</script>`, `</b/c>`) and anchors no path, so the token
  starts after it. `</script>` yields the lone name `script`, which names only a file called
  `script` (V1-0290) and never the directory `script/` from the repository root; `</b/c>` yields
  `./b/c/`, matched by component run like any unanchored run. Proof that the dropped units cannot
  observe the change: a tag name is markup text the unit writes or parses, not a path it opens,
  and a literal that opens a root-anchored path carries its `/` without a `<` before it. The
  other plugins and `tools/gate-affected-select` keep the wider rule and over-select only.
  Failure mode: a single literal that really spells a root path right after `<` loses its anchor
  and is observed only through rule (a), a declared read scope or rule (d); two literals joined
  at run time are unchanged. Falsifier: a unit whose only link to a dirty path is `</name>` in
  one literal and whose test reads that path. Rollback: remove the closing-tag branch of
  `literalTokens`.
- **AFP-V0-506:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2553) Amending the
  AFP-V0-021 lexicon and AFP-V0-012 rule (d) in the Go plugin: a backslash directly after a
  parent component (`..\x`) is read as the separator `../x` before tokenization, so the token is
  the whole climbing path and rule (d) judges where it resolves, instead of a bare `..` that
  reaches the repository root from any test file. A lone `".."` literal in a test file still marks
  its package. A `..` inside a comment is no literal (`go/scanner` skips comments) and never
  marks. Proof: `..\fixtures\x` from package `p` resolves to `fixtures/x`, inside the
  repository, exactly as `../fixtures/x` does, and both spellings match the same component runs.
  Failure mode: a drive or UNC prefix is unchanged. Rollback: remove `parentBackslash`.
- **AFP-V0-507:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2818) Amending
  AFP-V0-094: the `unsupported-affected-mixed-worktree` refusal of `--path` on a dirty worktree
  SHALL name its recovery in the message: `corvint affected` without `--path`, which plans the
  dirty worktree set now, or committing the dirty paths and running `corvint affected --base
  <HEAD>` with HEAD's full commit id, which plans every path committed after that HEAD together
  with any later worktree change. `--path` still never plans a dirty worktree, so it never
  under-selects by mixing bytes HEAD does not pin into HEAD's graph. This refusal with recovery
  is the 1.0 behaviour; a mixed-scope receipt is a non-goal (see "Non-goals and authority"). The
  refusal-cause line of `tools/unbounded-readers` is AFP-V0-505 (V1-2823), which also serves the
  V1-2553 ask that the refusal name the file and the literal or call. Rollback: remove
  `affectedPathListRecovery`.
- **AFP-V0-508:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2726) Amending
  AFP-V0-465 and AFP-V0-160 (6): this repository's declaration SHALL also name `docs/decisions/`
  and every top-level `docs/*.md` file, each file as its own entry, and
  `TestRepositoryDeclaresBuildLogDocumentation_AFPV0160` SHALL hold that every declared entry
  exists and that every top-level `docs/*.md` file is declared, so a new document is declared in
  the change that adds it and a stale entry is removed with its file. The AFP-V0-465 argument
  carries: a decision record or a top-level document is read only by the units whose path
  literals, declared read scopes or unbounded reads reach it, which AFP-V0-160 (3) still selects,
  and by repository gates outside any unit (`make capability-catalog`, the use-case receipts);
  no plugin claims it as source, so a change whose only unowned dirty paths are such documents
  takes no plugin into the plan and brings no plugin-wide `LANGUAGE_FRONTIER`. `docs/*.json`
  schemas, `docs/experiments/`, `docs/plans/`, `docs/post-merge-workflow/` and
  `docs/agent-memory/` stay undeclared, so a change to them stays `UNOWNED_DIRTY_PATH`. Failure
  modes and falsifier as AFP-V0-465; a document that becomes a build input is a declaration error
  the owner corrects. Rollback: restore the two-entry declaration and the test's expected list.
- **AFP-V0-509:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2833) Amending
  AFP-V0-094: one separated `--path` SHALL take every following argument up to the next argument
  that begins with `-` as a further path, so `--path a b c`, `--path a --path b --path c` and
  `--path=a --path b c` plan byte-identical documents; each value is held to the AFP-V0-094
  canonical-path rule and the 4096-path bound, the first value still refuses an option-looking
  name as `invalid-arguments`, and a trailing `--path` without a value still refuses. A word after
  an option that takes no further value (`--path a --full b`) is still `unrecognized arguments`.
  `corvint help affected` documents the form. Evidence: `TestAFPV0509PathTakesSeveralValues`.
  Falsifier: a `--path a b` document that differs from the `--path a --path b` document, or a
  value after `--path` that is refused only because it is not preceded by its own flag. Rollback:
  drop the loop after `addAffectedPath` in `parseAffectedOptions`; the help line reverts with it.
- **AFP-V0-510:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2837) The
  `affected-plan/1` default (and the review embedding of it, RGV-V0-015) MUST carry a top-level
  member `summary` `{dirty, excluded, line, scope, selected, unknown, unknownByReason}` restating
  the plan: `scope` is `plan.scope`; `dirty`, `selected` and `unknown` are the lengths of
  `plan.dirty`, `plan.selected` and `plan.unknown`; `excluded` is `plan.excluded.count`;
  `unknownByReason` maps each distinct `plan.unknown[].reason` to its count (an empty object when
  nothing is unknown); and `line` is the text
  `scope=S dirty=N selected=N excluded=N unknown=N` followed by one ` REASON=N` per reason in
  byte order. The summary never adds, removes or reorders a selection, and every other member is
  unchanged; `--full` (`affected-plan/0`) is byte-for-byte unchanged. Compatibility: an added
  member under CCF-V1-006; the `affected worktree` and `affected committed range` core-freeze
  goldens are regenerated for it. Non-goal: a flag or stderr line (`affected` has no `--format`,
  AFP-V0-035); receipt size (V1-2647). Evidence: `TestAFPV0510AffectedSummaryRestatesThePlan`.
  Falsifier: a summary count that differs from the plan member it restates, a reason missing
  from `unknownByReason`, a `line` that disagrees with the object, or a changed `--full`
  document. Rollback: drop `Summary` from `affectedCompactReceipt` and regenerate the goldens.
- **AFP-V0-511:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2726,
  V1-2708) Per-reason frontier reach for a dirty path no plugin owns. Before this requirement
  `participants` admitted every plugin once a dirty path was owned by none and was neither task
  state (AFP-V0-088) nor declared documentation (AFP-V0-160), so each plugin's whole plugin-wide
  frontier landed in `plan.unknown`: measured at `bebabe67e` with every plugin loaded, a `Makefile`
  edit or a Go `testdata` fixture (`internal/tasks/ticket/testdata/*.json`) carried 98
  `LANGUAGE_FRONTIER` entries (typescript 75, ruby 9, dotnet 4, swift 3, cfamily 2, python 2, go 2,
  shell 1), none of which named a path that could be the fixture. Rules:
  (1) A plugin MAY implement the optional interface `FrontierReach` with `SourceBoundFrontiers()
  []string`, `ResolutionInput(path string) bool` and `FixtureClosedFrontiers() []string`. A reason
  is the first two colon-separated fields of a frontier entry, `<plugin>:<category>`. `Build`
  validates both lists: every reason carries the declaring plugin's own namespace, a non-empty
  category and no further field, and no reason is on both lists; otherwise `Build` fails with
  `ErrInvalidLanguage`, so a malformed declaration can never narrow a plan. The declarations
  classify plugin code, not the observed repository, and are no part of the graph digest, so a
  plan's exclusion certificates stay comparable across them.
  (2) A plugin that does not implement the interface, and every reason a plugin does not name, is
  path-open (fail closed): the hidden edge may bind any path, so the reason bears on a dirty path
  no plugin owns. A source-bound reason is one whose hidden edge the plugin's own resolution binds
  only to a path its `Owns` claims, one of its units lists, or one the site's unit records as a
  path token, so that such a dirty path reaches the unit by AFP-V0-021 rule (d) instead; it bears
  on no dirty path that no plugin owns. A plugin that resolves against files it does not own, such
  as every repository file (`cfamily` resolves quoted includes against the whole tree), MUST NOT
  implement the interface.
  (3) For a plugin that owns no dirty path, reads no path (`PathReader`) and has no reached unit,
  `frontierUnknowns` raises only its path-open entries, except that a dirty path the plugin
  reports as a `ResolutionInput` (a manifest, build or test-runner configuration, or a foreign
  asset its `Units` pass to `Source.Files`, judged on the base name) keeps every entry of that
  plugin, because a change there may rebind any site it resolved. A plugin that owns a dirty path,
  reads any path or has a reached unit raises every entry, as before.
  (4) Go-fixture rule: when every dirty path lies below a `testdata` directory a Go unit hosts
  (AFP-V0-446 rule (i)), and a non-participating plugin lists no path below any of those
  directories, that plugin's `FixtureClosedFrontiers` entries are dropped when their site is a
  located path outside every such directory; a bound (`+N`) entry, a bare entry and a site inside a
  fixture directory are kept, and one dirty path outside every hosted fixture turns the rule off
  for the whole dirty set. A fixture-closed reason is a path-open reason whose hidden edge binds a
  test runner, a build graph between the plugin's own units, or a file in an ancestor directory of
