  the site's own, never a file below a foreign `testdata` directory.
  (5) `UNOWNED_DIRTY_PATH` is unchanged, so `plan.scope` stays `UNKNOWN`; `frontierSites`
  (AFP-V0-381) counts the repository and is unchanged. The AFP-V0-028 allowlist already admits
  every non-Go `LANGUAGE_FRONTIER` entry, so `corvint test` selection cannot change; the
  deliverable is a plan whose unknowns can bear on it, smaller receipts (V1-2647) and a reviewable
  frontier. Declarations at acceptance: typescript source-bound `relative-import-unresolved`,
  `path-alias-unresolved`, fixture-closed `test-runner-unknown`, `test-runner-ambiguous`, inputs
  its `observedName` manifests and configs; python source-bound `conditional-import`,
  `dynamic-import`, `relative-import-unresolved`, `pytest-plugins-unresolved`, no inputs; ruby
  source-bound `relative-load-unresolved`, `rails-autoload`, `dynamic-load`, `conditional-load`,
  `ambiguous-load`, fixture-closed `ambiguous-test-framework` (unit-level only, so never raised
  plugin-wide), inputs `Rakefile` and `.feature`; dotnet source-bound `unparsed-source`,
  fixture-closed `dynamic-test-discovery`, `test-runner-filter-unresolved`, inputs the project
  files, `Directory.Build.props/targets` and `.feature`; swift source-bound
  `conditional-compilation`, fixture-closed `dynamic-test-discovery`, `test-framework-unresolved`,
  inputs its `metadataFile` manifests (which include `package.json`).
  `dotnet:source-project-unresolved`, `swift:unmapped-source`, every computed-import and runtime
  reason of typescript, and the go, shell and cfamily reasons stay path-open. Measured at
  `bebabe67e` with the lane build: `Makefile` 98 to 69 entries,
  `internal/tasks/ticket/testdata/issue502-escalation-hold-cases.json` 98 to 84 (typescript takes
  part through a path-literal reader unit), `extensions/vscode/tsconfig.json` 98 to 86 and
  `extensions/vscode/package.json` 98 to 87 (both resolution inputs). Proof
  obligation per declaring plugin, modelled on the AFP-V0-446 tests: a test that an unowned path (a
  `Makefile`, a Go fixture) raises exactly the path-open entries, that a resolution input and a
  dirty path the plugin owns still raise every entry, and that the dirty files leave the plugin's
  `Units` result unchanged. Non-goal: narrowing a selection (the `UNBOUNDED_READER` set, V1-0643,
  V1-0883). Rollback: the interface is optional, so a plugin that names nothing behaves as before.

### Weekly flake report and failed-shard typed reasons (AFP-V0-530, AFP-V0-531; V1-2682, V1-2689)

Intent (owner, chat 2026-10-27: "ci-control-plane OK: V1-2682 option A, weekly flake-report
workflow plus the V1-2689 FAIL-line job summary"). AFP-V0-441 turns a shard whose every failed
invocation passed its one rerun into a FLAKY pass and retains the record, but nothing reads those
records together, so a test that flakes every week is only ever seen one run at a time. And a shard
that still fails (V1-2689: four box shards on 2026-10-25 failed the Go step with no failing test in
the stream) leaves the reader to open the raw log to learn why. Both are reporting: no gate, tier,
rerun policy or status changes.

- **AFP-V0-530:** The weekly flake report. The workflow `.github/workflows/flake-report.yml` runs
  on a Monday schedule and on `workflow_dispatch`, only from `main`, with workflow permissions
  `contents: read` and a single job holding `actions: read`, `contents: read` and `issues: write`
  (declared in `script/ci-least-privilege-exceptions.tsv`), a `cancel-in-progress: false`
  concurrency group, a `persist-credentials: false` checkout and pinned actions. Its only script,
  `.github/flake-report/report.sh`, (1) lists with `gh api --paginate` the artifacts named
  `ci-flaky-<event>-<shard>` for `pull_request` and `merge_group` and shard indices below
  `FLAKE_REPORT_SHARDS` (default 16, at least the AFP-V0-441 matrix width); (2) selects, with
  `tools/ci-flake-report select`, the unexpired ones of at most 1 MiB created in the window
  `[now - 7 days, now)`, once each; (3) downloads each archive with `gh api .../artifacts/<id>/zip`,
  and (4) aggregates them with `tools/ci-flake-report report`: it reads `flaky.json`
  (`corvint-ci-flaky/0`, `verdict` `FLAKY`) out of each archive and prints one Markdown report with
  the window, the counts of FLAKY shard passes, distinct CI runs and passes per event, one row per
  `<package>.<test>` (per `<package>` when the record names no test) with its FLAKY passes, distinct
  runs, per-event counts and first and last day seen, most frequent first, at most 200 rows, and a
  `Not read` list naming every selected record it could not download or read (at most 50 named):
  a lost record widens the uncertainty, never lowers the rate. (5) The report is appended to the job
  summary and written in place into the body of the oldest open non-pull-request issue labelled
  `ci-flake-report` (the label is ensured first); only when none exists is one created, titled
  `CI flake report (weekly, AFP-V0-530)`, and pinned, a refused pin (GitHub pins at most three
  issues) being reported in the summary rather than failing the run. A listing failure, a tool
  failure or an issue write failure fails the run with a `::error` annotation; a download failure
  only warns and is named in the report. The tool reads files only, never the network.
- **AFP-V0-531:** Failed-shard typed reasons. In the Go tests step of `go-product-shard`, after
  the AFP-V0-441 FLAKY decision and before the step exits with the shard's status,
  `.github/forward-rows/failed.sh STREAM STATUS` runs only when the status is still nonzero and the
  stream was captured, under `|| true`, so it can neither fail the step nor rescue it. From the
  `go test -json` stream (`failed.jq`; non-JSON lines are ignored) it appends to the job summary a
  `### FAILED: go-product-shard S of N exited X` section with one typed reason per package that
  reported a package-level `fail` or `build-fail` event: `test-failures` (naming its failed tests,
  at most 50), `build-failed` (`[build failed]`, `[setup failed]` or a `build-fail` event),
  `timeout` (`panic: test timed out`), `signal` (a `signal: ` line), `panic` (`panic: ` or
  `fatal error: `), else `package-fail-no-test`; the packages that started but never finished (at
  most 20); and the stream's `--- FAIL`, `FAIL`, `panic:`, `fatal error:`, `exit status` and
  `signal:` lines in a fenced block (at most 200). When no package reported a failure it says so:
  the Go step ended without a failure report (killed, out of memory, a runner signal or a truncated
  stream), which is the V1-2689 shape. An unreadable stream is said in the summary. It prints one
  `::error` annotation naming the shard, the status and the reasons. The uncaptured path (no
  outcomes directory) gets no section.

Non-goals: V1-2682 options B (a rerun budget per test) and C (quarantine lists); any change to a
gate, a tier, the AFP-V0-047 rerun or the AFP-V0-441 FLAKY decision; filing or closing tickets
from the report; reading the `go test` log of a passing shard (none is retained); a report for
`push` runs (AFP-V0-441 retains no record for them); naming the person who caused a flake.

Failure modes: see `## Failure modes`.

Falsifier: any of the following refutes these requirements:
- a report whose counts exclude a selected record without naming it under `Not read`;
- a second `ci-flake-report` issue created while one is open;
- a workflow run on a pull request, or a job token broader than the declared exception;
- a `failed.sh` exit that changes the shard's status in either direction, or a FLAKY pass whose
  summary carries a FAILED section;
- a stream with a package-level `fail` event and no typed reason for that package.

Rollback: delete `.github/workflows/flake-report.yml`, `.github/flake-report/`,
`tools/ci-flake-report/` and the `job:report` exception row; remove the `failed.sh` line of the Go
tests step, `.github/forward-rows/failed.sh` and `failed.jq`, and the `TestAFPV0530*` and
`TestAFPV0531*` tests. Close or unpin the report issue by hand. Nothing else reads either output.

### Pull-request shard parallelism (AFP-V0-540, AFP-V0-541; V1-2890)

Intent (V1-2890): a pull-request `go-product-shard` took 22 to 41 minutes on the self-hosted box
(run 40544500978). Compilation was 2 to 3 minutes of each shard; the rest was package tests run
one at a time and, on the shards that hold them, two test slices run one after another after the
whole packages (shard 7: 15.2 and 10.1 minutes; shard 4: 18.4 and 6.4 minutes). The pull-request
run is advisory to the merge queue, which runs the full suite before any merge, so its wall time
is the one to cut, without changing the merge queue's deciding run or the AFP-V0-014 profile.

- **AFP-V0-540:** (accepted by the owner (owner chat, 2026-10-27: "I approve the ci.yml change for that scope, keep -race and leave the AFP-V0-014 qualification argv as-is"); V1-2890)
  The `go-product-shard` Go tests step SHALL take its `go test -p` value from `SHARD_TEST_P`,
  which is `4` only when the event is `pull_request` and the runner environment is `self-hosted`,
  and `1` on every other run. There are five parts.
  - (1) The whole-package, slice and AFP-V0-047 rerun invocations all take `-p "$SHARD_TEST_P"`
    and otherwise keep `-exec "$RUNNER_TEMP/test-confine" -json -count=1 -race -timeout 50m`,
    the AFP-V0-023 confinement and the job environment. `-race` and `-count=1` never change.
  - (2) A `merge_group` shard, the deciding full suite and the only AFP-V0-428 seed producer,
    and a hosted shard keep `-p 1`, so the ci profile of `STR-V0-012` (`ciGoFlags`, `-p 1`)
    still equals the merge queue's argv, and hosted AFP-V0-040 cost streams stay unshared.
  - (3) The AFP-V0-014 historical qualification argv (`go test -json -p 1 -count=1 -race
    -timeout 50m` in `tools/corvint-pr-tests`) is unchanged. No CI shard produces AFP-V0-014 or
    AFP-V0-015 evidence: that profile runs only in its own driver campaign.
  - (4) AFP-V0-029 forward rows stay comparable. A pull-request row compares the package sets S
    and F of the PR shards and the queue run; no row, collector or classifier reads the argv,
    `-p` or event times. `CORVINT_PR_FORWARD_EPOCH` does not advance. Within one PR run a
    narrowed and a whole shard take the same `SHARD_TEST_P`, so AFP-V0-028's argv match still
    holds between them.
  - (5) Caching is unchanged: a pull-request shard still only reads the per-runner AFP-V0-427
    seed and never writes it or the hosted main cache.
  Non-goals: changing the merge-queue argv, the shard count or partition, the AFP-V0-014 or
  AFP-V0-015 profiles, `CORVINT_PR_NARROWING`, or any test. Failure modes: more concurrent race
  binaries on the shared 32-thread host can raise CPU and memory pressure; a test that fails
  only under that contention fails the PR shard, is rerun once by AFP-V0-441 and is recorded
  FLAKY or failed in the forward row, and the merge queue still decides at `-p 1`. Rollback:
  revert the V1-2890 commit; `SHARD_TEST_P` and `run_slice` go away and every shard runs `-p 1`
  with sequential slices.
- **AFP-V0-541:** (accepted by the owner (owner chat, 2026-10-27); V1-2890)
  When `SHARD_TEST_P` is above 1 and a shard's slices name distinct packages, `run_tests` SHALL
  start each slice in the background, writing its stdout and stderr to its own files in a fresh
  directory under `RUNNER_TEMP`, before the whole-package invocation; after the whole packages it
  SHALL wait for each slice in slice order, replay its stdout into the AFP-V0-040 capture and its
  stderr to the log, and append each failed slice once to `failed-slices`. There are three parts.
  - (1) The capture keeps AFP-V0-041(7)'s layout: the whole packages first, then each slice in
    slice order, one terminal outcome per package, inside the one capture.
  - (2) Any failed invocation still fails the shard, and a whole-package failure still writes
    `whole-failed`; the AFP-V0-441 rerun and FLAKY decision read the same files.
  - (3) With `-p 1`, two slices of one package, or a stream directory that cannot be made, the
    slices run one after another after the whole packages, as before.
  Non-goals: running slices of one package together, which would share one package directory;
  live log output of a slice before it ends. Failure modes: a slice that hangs is stopped by its
  own `-timeout 50m` and the job timeout, as before; an unreadable stdout stream file fails that
  slice, and its stderr file is replayed best effort. Rollback: as `AFP-V0-540`.

### Frontier participation scoped to the change (AFP-V0-560, V1-2708)

Intent (V1-2708): on this repository every change that reached a TypeScript or shell script test
only as a reader of a changed path (for example `cmd/corvint/main.go`, which the TypeScript host
tests name) made the TypeScript plugin take part, so its 75 plugin-wide frontier reasons, none of
which can reach the change, kept the plan `UNKNOWN`. Product invariant 2 still binds: a frontier
reason may be left out of `plan.unknown` only for a stated, tested reason that the change cannot
reach any edge it hides, and it is then recorded, never dropped silently.

- **AFP-V0-560:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2708)
  Amending `AFP-V0-004`'s rule that a plugin takes part in a plan when it owns a reached unit, a
  reached unit SHALL NOT by itself make its plugin take part when its witness is a terminal
  selection: `PATH_LITERAL_READER`, `PATH_GLOB_READER`, `DECLARED_READ_SCOPE` or `BINARY_EXEC`,
  and `testlessReaderUsers` (AFP-V0-495) did not traverse it. An `UNBOUNDED_READER` is no terminal
  selection: the unbounded set (`AFP-V0-012` rule (d), `AFP-V0-192`) is the traversal closure of
  the root locators and their test users, so a hidden edge into a locator could add a member the
  plan misses, and its plugin takes part. There are five parts.
  - (1) Every other participation rule of `AFP-V0-004` is unchanged: a plugin still takes part
    when it owns or claims a dirty path, owns a reached unit with any other witness or one
    `testlessReaderUsers` traversed, or reads any path (`affected.PathReader`). Once a dirty path
    is owned by none (and is neither task state nor declared documentation), this rule is off: a
    plugin with a terminal reached unit takes part and raises every entry, as a plugin with a
    reached unit does under `AFP-V0-511` (3), and every other plugin raises the entries
    `AFP-V0-511` names. Where the two rules meet, the plan keeps the wider frontier.
  - (2) A reached unit's own `frontier` member (V1-0289) is still named by every plan that reaches
    it, whatever its witness.
  - (3) For each plugin left out only by this rule whose plugin-wide reasons are not otherwise
    named, the plan MUST carry one `plan.frontierExcluded` entry
    `{"language","reason","frontier","units"}`: the plugin, the fixed reason
    `FrontierExclusionReason`, its sorted plugin-wide reasons, and the sorted terminal units that
    reached it. Entries are sorted by language. The member is additive and omitted when empty, so
    a plan with no such plugin is byte-identical to the one before this requirement; the compact
    projection (`affectedCompactPlan`) carries it unchanged.
  - (4) Soundness. Under `AFP-V0-004`, a plugin-wide reason hides an edge or unit of that plugin:
    an edge from one of its units, or an edge into it from an unowned path. A terminal selection
    is never traversed to its dependents (`AFP-V0-021`, `-023`, `-037`, `-401`, `-495` (2)), and
    each is a direct match of the unit itself (its own path tokens, globs, declared scope or
    `execs` naming a reached command), not a closure over edges, so resolving any
    hidden edge out of or into it cannot add a selection. A hidden edge
    from a changed path into the plugin needs that path to be the plugin's own or unowned, and
    either makes the plugin take part by (1). Leaving the reasons out therefore changes no
    selection the frontier could hide.
  - (5) Selection, exclusions, seeds, token bounds and every other unknown are unchanged; only
    which `LANGUAGE_FRONTIER` entries are named and the new member change.
  Non-goals: narrowing the unowned-path rule (`AFP-V0-511`) further; hidden
  cross-language edges, such as a TypeScript end-to-end test that drives a Go server
  (`typescript:e2e-runtime-dependency`), or a path literal inside a source the plugin could not
  read, which `AFP-V0-004` already does not model when no unit of the plugin is reached, so this
  rule only removes coincidental coverage of them through a reader; scoping `go:nested-module-frontier` to the packages the nested module imports
  (`tools/local-authority` requires the root module through `replace`, a real cross-module edge);
  and the Go and shell unit frontiers of reached units. Failure modes: a plugin whose model puts a
  hidden edge from a changed Go path into one of its terminal readers' dependents would be left
  out wrongly; no plugin's model has such an edge, and a plugin whose units can read any path
  takes part by (1). A unit wrongly given a terminal witness although it was traversed would
  leave its plugin out; `carried` from `testlessReaderUsers` is the only traversal that keeps an
  earlier witness. A consumer that reads `plan.unknown` for its own decision also sees the
  reasons left out: `internal/appflows` treats a non-e2e TypeScript reason as making its e2e
  selection unbounded, so a change that reaches TypeScript only through a terminal reader no
  longer forces that path; this is the same coincidental coverage of a hidden cross-language
  edge, and the e2e frontiers it keys on stay exempt there. Falsifier: a plan that leaves a plugin out under this rule while some hidden
  edge of it, once resolved, adds a selection. Acceptance:
  `TestUnrelatedDynamicImportDoesNotWidenAnotherLanguagesChange_AFP_V0_560`,
  `TestChangeTheDynamicImportCanReachStaysUnknown_AFP_V0_560`,
  `TestChangeInTheFrontierPluginsParticipantSetStaysUnknown_AFP_V0_560`,
  `TestTraversedTestlessReaderKeepsItsPluginsFrontier_AFP_V0_560`,
  `TestUnboundedReaderClosureKeepsItsPluginsFrontier_AFP_V0_560`,
  `TestUnownedPathKeepsTerminalReadersPluginWholeFrontier_AFP_V0_560_AFP_V0_511`,
  `TestAFPV0560CompactPlanKeepsFrontierExcluded`; measured in
  `https://github.com/beamfall/corvint-history/blob/1c4d8793cf71cbb01b8509b68a6cb1502a40a13e/docs/build-log/2026-10-27-frontier-change-scoping.md`. Rollback: make `terminalWitness` return
  false, then remove `Plan.FrontierExcluded`, `FrontierExclusion` and the compact member; every
  reached unit's plugin takes part again.

### Scoped unit frontiers (AFP-V0-580 to AFP-V0-582; V1-2896, V1-2782, V1-2897)

Intent (V1-2896, V1-2782, V1-2897; baseline V1-2892, 0 of 50 Corvint lane changes `BOUNDED`):
`go:build-constraint-variants`, `go:nested-module-frontier` and `shell:foreign-invocation-unmodelled`
were named by every Corvint plan, because a unit frontier (`AFP-V0-004`, V1-0289) bore on any plan
that reached its unit and the nested-module frontier was plugin-wide. Each stays named whenever the
condition it describes can bear on the change (invariant 2); a plan that reaches the unit only in a
way the condition cannot affect no longer inherits it.

- **AFP-V0-580:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2896)
  The Go plugin MUST raise `go:build-constraint-variants` for a package any of whose files only
  some builds compile: a file with a `//go:build` line or with a GOOS/GOARCH file-name suffix as
  `go/build` reads it (`_windows.go`, `_linux_arm64_test.go`). The frontier bears on every plan that
  reaches the package (`AFP-V0-004`), because a variant file can call any of the package's code and
  share its process-wide state, so any change that reaches the package can change what a variant
  does. Non-goals: evaluating build constraints for a host platform; scoping the frontier to the
  variant file's own imports or path literals, which was implemented and withdrawn in review as
  unsound (a variant calling a plain file's function would lose the frontier when that function
  changed). Failure modes: a suffix-only variant package that base detection missed now names the
  frontier. Rollback: drop `hasPlatformSuffix` from the variant test.
- **AFP-V0-581:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2897)
  Amending `AFP-V0-004`'s rule that a unit frontier bears on every plan that reaches its unit, a
  unit MAY declare `frontierBears: DEPENDENTS`: its frontier is named only on a plan that follows
  the unit's dependents or the unit's own code reads a dirty path. There are four parts.
  - (1) The plan follows a unit's dependents when the reverse-dependency traversal expands it (a
    dirty path it owns, or a dependency reached), when it is a test-less reader of an unowned
    dirty path (`AFP-V0-495`), or, on any dirty path, when it locates the root (`AFP-V0-012`
    rule (d)).
  - (2) A unit selected as a reader of a dirty path (`PATH_LITERAL_READER` or `PATH_GLOB_READER`,
    `AFP-V0-021`), owned or not, with or without tests, is not followed but its frontier is named:
    the unit's own code may read the path, so what it does can change with the path and so may
    what its unmodelled dependents do, and the plan selects none of them (amended 2026-10-27,
    batch review: the first text left a reader of an owned path unnamed, which turned a plan that
    excluded a Go test running a shell script that reads the dirty Go file from `UNKNOWN` into
    `BOUNDED`). A narrowed reader (`AFP-V0-072` (4)) bears the same way, so the narrowing removes
    no entry. A unit selected only as a test user, an executable user (`AFP-V0-037`, a declaration
    of what its tests run), an own-reason unbounded reader that does not locate the root (the
    cause is then in a test file, `LocatesRoot`) or a declared-scope reader (`AFP-V0-023`, its
    tests' reads) is selected for its tests alone, so its frontier is not named. Bearing is decided
    by whether the unit's own path literal resolves to, or its own glob matches, a dirty path (or the
    literal names one and is narrowed), not by which witness won: a unit reached first as a test
    user, an executable user or any other selection whose own literal or glob also names a dirty
    path bears as a reader does (amended 2026-10-27, batch review: the first rule keyed bearing to
    the winning witness, so a test user that also read the dirty path dropped its frontier and the
    plan could come back `BOUNDED` with its dependents' tests excluded).
  - (3) The shell plugin declares it when `shell:foreign-invocation-unmodelled` (`NSA-V0-012`) is
    the unit's only frontier, because the frontier hides only the unit's invokers.
  - (4) Only the absent value and `DEPENDENTS` are valid; any other value is an invalid unit. The
    field is in the graph digest and the graph cache.
  With `AFP-V0-560`: a reached unit the plan follows the dependents of is no terminal selection,
  so its plugin takes part even when its witness is a terminal reader (a root locator or a unit
  the root locators reach that is also selected as a path reader). This rule narrows only unit
  frontiers; `AFP-V0-560` narrows only plugin-wide reasons and records them in
  `plan.frontierExcluded`; a unit frontier this rule leaves unnamed is not recorded there.
  Non-goals: modelling the invokers. Failure modes: a root-locating script bears on every dirty
  path. Rollback: drop `FrontierBears`; the frontier bears on reach again.
- **AFP-V0-582:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2782)
  Amending `AFP-V0-031`, a nested module that stays open only because its own manifest requires,
  replaces or names a tool under an observed module path, or replaces a module with a directory
  of an observed listed module of the same path, MUST become the synthetic unit
  `go:<module path>` instead of raising the plugin-wide `go:nested-module-frontier`. There are
  four parts.
  - (1) The unit holds no source and no test. It imports the observed packages the module's files
    import, the observed packages its manifest names as `tool`, and the observed packages any of
    its string literals names (as `go run` or `go test` arguments do). It carries their path
    tokens and literal globs, is `UnboundedReads` and `LocatesRoot` when a file locates the root
    or holds a literal that escapes its package, and carries the unscoped unit frontier
    `go:nested-module-frontier`.
  - (2) The frontier is therefore named exactly when the plan reaches the unit: an imported,
    tool-named or literal-named observed package or a path the module's literals name changed, or
    the module can read any path. A change to the module's own files stays outside every unit and
    is `UNKNOWN` as before.
  - (3) The plugin-wide frontier stays when any file of the module cannot be read, parsed or
    lexed; when the synthetic id collides with an observed unit; when a `go.work` sits above it;
    when the observed side draws it in; when a directory replacement resolves to a module other
    than the one it replaces; or when a module replacement aliases an observed module path.
  - (4) The unit is a unit like any other for the graph digest, the graph cache and `excluded`.
  Non-goals: selecting the nested module's own tests. Failure modes: an unread edge keeps the
  plugin-wide frontier. Rollback: drop `dependentModuleUnits`; the open module raises the
  plugin-wide frontier again.

### Balanced narrowed shards (AFP-V0-590, AFP-V0-591; V1-2942)

Intent (V1-2942, owner: "ensure we are only running the tests we need, split intelligently across
shards"): a narrowed pull-request run intersected the selection with the full-universe partition
(`AFP-V0-028`), so a selection that fell mostly into one shard's partition ran on that shard alone
while the other seven finished empty. The selection is now placed across the fixed eight shards by
the same measured costs, and go-product refuses any run in which that placement could leave a
selected package unrun. The full fallback is unchanged.

- **AFP-V0-590:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2942)
  Amending `AFP-V0-028` and `AFP-V0-098`, a `NARROWED` shard SHALL run its part of the
  cost-balanced placement of the driver selection. There are four parts.
  - (1) `cishards.Select` (`SelectFrom`) keeps the selected packages that are in the complete
    universe, once each, and places only them with `PlanFrom`: the same longest-processing-time
    placement, the median of the whole cost table for an unpriced package, and the same
    `AFP-V0-041` slices for a split package. Every selected package of the universe lands on exactly
    one shard (whole or as all of its slices), none is omitted, a package outside the universe is
    dropped, an empty selection leaves every shard empty, and selecting the whole universe or `./...`
    is exactly the full plan. The helper takes it as `--select FILE`, with `--slices` and `--order`.
  - (2) `narrow.sh` writes the selection that is in the universe, checking each stage on its own
    (the shell has no `pipefail`), places it with `ci-shards --select`, and `narrow.jq` records
    `placement: "BALANCED"`, `selected` (that selection) and `executed` (this shard's placed
    packages and split packages). The record is `FULL` with `placement: "PARTITION"` and an empty
    `selected`, recording the reason, when a selection stage fails, the recorded selection is not
    exactly the driver selection intersected with the universe (passed to `narrow.jq`), the
    driver selection is non-empty but names no package of the universe, or the placement fails or
    names a package outside the selection. A truncated or empty selection therefore never
    narrows. `skipped` stays the shard's
    partition packages outside the selection, which `row.jq` (`AFP-V0-099`) reads unchanged.
  - (3) The ci.yml tests step recomputes the placement of a `BALANCED` record's `selected` with the
    workflow's own helper and runs it only when it equals the record's `executed`. When it
    disagrees the shard runs its whole partition and its placed part; when it cannot be
    recomputed, or the record has no valid `selected`, the shard runs whole as a `PARTITION`
    shard. A record without `placement` (an earlier `narrow.jq`) keeps the `AFP-V0-098`
    intersection.
  - (4) The matrix stays eight shards; a shard with no placed package runs no Go test and ends
    in seconds, as an `AFP-V0-028` empty intersection does.
  Non-goals: changing the full, `merge_group` or `main` push partition; changing the matrix
  size; measuring costs anew. Failure modes: an outdated cost table unbalances the placement
  (advisory `AFP-V0-040` drift report); a re-pinned `CORVINT_PR_TOOL_SOURCE` is needed before a
  record says `BALANCED`. Rollback: revert the `narrow.sh` and `narrow.jq` change and its pin,
  which restores `PARTITION` records; the ci.yml path and guard then pass unchanged.
- **AFP-V0-591:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2942)
  Coverage guard. Because a balanced shard runs another shard's partition packages, a run that
  mixes a balanced shard with one that ran only its partition could leave a selected package
  unrun. Every pull-request shard whose tests step ran SHALL retain a `corvint-pr-coverage/0`
  marker as `ci-coverage-<shard index>` (14 days, overwritten by a rerun, the upload failing the
  shard when it cannot): run id and attempt, shard, shard count, the placement it ran (`BALANCED`
  only when it ran its recomputed part) and, for `BALANCED`, the selection. The required
  go-product check SHALL, on a `pull_request` whose shards succeeded, fetch the markers and fail
  closed, with an error ending "Re-run all jobs.", unless the markers name exactly shards 0 to
  N-1 once each (N the matrix length, the go-product `SHARDS`), all are `corvint-pr-coverage/0`
  of this run id with N shards and an attempt no later than the current one, and either all are
  `PARTITION` (every shard ran its partition or the intersection with it, `AFP-V0-028`) or all are
  `BALANCED` with the same selection. No marker, a missing, repeated or extra shard, an unknown
  placement, unreadable markers or a mix fail. Only a docs-plan `DOCS` or `REUSE` run, whose
  shards run no tests step, needs no marker. Attempts: a marker of an earlier attempt of this run
  counts, since each shard keeps the latest marker it uploaded (overwritten by a rerun) for the
  same tested merge, and the agreement rule admits a mix of attempts only when every shard ran
  its partition or its part of one placement of one selection. Non-goals: any change to
  `merge_group`, which runs full and reads no marker. Failure modes: a partial rerun of one failed
  shard after its peers fell back fails the guard (re-run all jobs); expired or deleted markers,
  or a marker that cannot be fetched, fail go-product. Rollback:
  remove the marker step, the fetch step and the guard block; with `AFP-V0-590` rolled back first
  no marker is ever `BALANCED`.
- **AFP-V0-600:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2941)
  The Go plugin records the files each package embeds. Before this requirement a `//go:embed`
  target was a dirty path no unit lists: rule (b) (`goDataRule`) already selected the embedding
  package and its dependents, but the plan also carried `UNOWNED_DIRTY_PATH` and, through
  AFP-V0-511, every other plugin's path-open frontier (measured at `5a354090d`:
  `internal/capcatalog/catalog.json` 70 unknown entries, 69 of them `LANGUAGE_FRONTIER`). Rules:
  (1) The Go plugin resolves each package's non-test `//go:embed` patterns as the go command does
  and records the matches, sorted, in `Unit.EmbedFiles`. A directive is a line comment that starts
  its line; its arguments are space-separated patterns, each optionally a Go string literal, with
  an optional `all:` prefix. A pattern is matched component by component relative to the package
  directory with `path.Match`; a matched file is embedded; a matched directory embeds every file
  below it except under a name beginning with `.` or `_` below the match (unless `all:`), under a
  version-control name (`.git`, `.hg`, `.svn`, `.bzr`) or below a nested module root.
  (2) Fail closed: a file that does not scan, a directive whose arguments do not parse, a pattern
  the go command rejects (`.`, a parent or empty element, a leading or trailing slash, a bad glob),
  a package whose target list is empty or over `MaxPathsPerUnit`, and every `_test.go` directive
  resolve nothing, so those files keep the earlier behaviour (rule (b) selection plus
  `UNOWNED_DIRTY_PATH`). Claiming less leaves a path unowned; it never selects less.
  (3) `Build` validates `EmbedFiles` like every unit path list; it is part of the graph digest and
  the graph cache. An embedded path is no plugin's source: `claimantsOf` names none for it, so the
  AFP-V0-560 terminal rule stays off and every other plugin keeps its AFP-V0-511 frontier and
  dynamic-reader entries for it, because a foreign test (a Jest `require(process.env.X)`) may load
  it by a computed name. It drops its `UNOWNED_DIRTY_PATH` entry only by AFP-V0-601, which also
  keeps it for an embedded driver input, or by the repository's declaration (AFP-V0-602). Selection is unchanged: rule (b) still selects and
  traverses the embedding package.
  Failure modes: a directive the scanner misreads as one could list a path the package does not
  embed; that only selects the package. Falsifier: a repository whose `go list -json`
  `EmbedFiles` for a package differ from `Unit.EmbedFiles`. Rollback: stop setting `EmbedFiles`;
  every embedded path is selected by rule (b) only.
- **AFP-V0-601:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2941)
  A dirty path no unit lists drops its `UNOWNED_DIRTY_PATH` entry only when every test process that
  could read it is accounted for. Finding one reader proves nothing about the rest: an undeclared
  Go test can open any file through `os.ReadFile(os.Getenv("FIXTURE_PATH"))`, which no literal or
  root-locator rule resolves. Rules:
  (1) The path is named by a Go reader the plan selects: a `//go:embed` target (AFP-V0-600), a file
  below a `testdata` directory a Go unit hosts (AFP-V0-446 rule (i); rule (b) selects the host), an
  entry or declared subtree of a read scope (AFP-V0-023), or the subtree of a read-scoped unit's
  own directory other than the repository root (`scopedReadersOf` selects the unit).
  (2) Every possible reader is confined (`confinedReaders`): every unit in the graph is a Go unit,
  so no other plugin's test can reach the path by a computed name, and every Go unit with tests
  either declares a read scope or encloses the path's directory, which rule (b) selects. Under
  AFP-V0-023 a declared package's tests run in full CI confined to their declared scope, so an
  undeclared read there fails instead of passing on an unselected edit. A graph with any other
  plugin's unit, or one Go package with tests and no declaration that does not enclose the path,
  keeps the entry, and the plan stays `UNKNOWN`.
  (3) Never attributed, even when embedded or declared: a build or CI driver input (`Makefile`,
  `makefile`, `GNUmakefile` or a `.mk` file at any depth, a path under `.github/workflows/` or
  `.github/actions/`), because an executor outside the graph consumes it; this check runs before
  every rule in (1). A task store projection path (AFP-V0-088) and a declared documentation path
  (AFP-V0-160) keep their own non-source class.
  (4) Attribution removes only the `UNOWNED_DIRTY_PATH` entry. The path is still no plugin's
  source, so `claimantsOf` names none and the AFP-V0-511 frontier and reader rules apply to it as
  to any unowned path. There is no frontier subsumption for unowned paths: the TypeScript
  Playwright declaration (TJAA-V0-121) and the app-flow closure treat an `UNOWNED_DIRTY_PATH` entry
  as bounded but a `LANGUAGE_FRONTIER` entry as unbounded, so dropping frontier entries in favour
  of the `UNOWNED` entry would widen their decisions.
  Failure modes, inherited from AFP-V0-023: Landlock does not mediate stat, access or existence
  checks, and local or AFP-V0-013 driver runs are not confined, so a declared package that only
  tests for a file's existence, or a run outside full CI, can miss an excluded edit. A repository
  in which the condition holds is Go-only with every unenclosing tester declared; Corvint itself
  has non-Go units and unconfined Go testers, so none of its paths is attributed by this rule; the
  project-owned declaration of AFP-V0-602 is the authority that classifies such paths. Falsifier: a dirty
  attributed path whose test outcome changes in a package the plan excludes. Rollback: drop the
  `goReadData` check in `seed`; the paths raise `UNOWNED_DIRTY_PATH` again.
- **AFP-V0-602:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2941,
  V1-2726) A project-owned declaration of the data paths whose every runtime reader is a unit the
  reader rules already name. AFP-V0-601 attributes nothing in a repository with any non-Go unit,
  because no rule can prove that a foreign test does not load a path by a computed name; only the
  project can state that (invariant 3). Before this requirement every batch of this repository
  carried such paths as `UNOWNED_DIRTY_PATH`, and through AFP-V0-511 each non-participating
  plugin's path-open frontier: measured on batch 1028c (`c6c75440bf2c..5cc0d42e84ff`, the
  `origin/main` binary) 10 `UNOWNED_DIRTY_PATH` (eight sealed lane CEMs,
  `.corvint/test-read-scopes.json`, the embedded `internal/capcatalog/catalog.json`) and 86
  `LANGUAGE_FRONTIER` (typescript 75, dotnet 3, cfamily 2, go 2, swift 2, ruby 1, shell 1). Rules:
  (1) `.corvint/runtime-readers.json`, profile `corvint-runtime-readers/0`, closed members
  `profile` and `entries`; each entry is `{path, reason}`. A path is a repository-relative file, or
  a directory when it ends in `/` (the AFP-V0-023 entry grammar); entries are strictly ascending by
  path, at most 256, in at most 64 KiB. A reason is non-empty, at most 512 bytes of UTF-8 with no
  control character, and states which readers the project relies on. No entry may be `.corvint/`,
  either declaration file or a directory holding one, `.github/` or anything at or under it, or a
  make file (AFP-V0-601 (3)); a make file below a declared directory stays unowned per path. Another
  `.corvint/` file may be declared one by one. An entry asserts that no test outside the units the
  reader rules name reads the path; a repository-wide check that CI runs unconditionally (a lint, a
  conformance validator, a release gate) is no test selection and does not contradict it. Any violation, or an unreadable or non-regular file, fails the graph
  build, so a malformed declaration can never classify a path. The entries are part of the graph
  digest when present (absent from the canonical form otherwise) and of the graph cache entry,
  whose stamps must include the declaration path, so an edit to it invalidates the entry.
  (2) After the reader rules have run, and after the task-state (AFP-V0-088) and documentation
  (AFP-V0-160) classes, each remaining `UNOWNED_DIRTY_PATH` entry whose path an entry holds, and
  which is no driver input, is removed and listed in `nonSource` as class
  `DECLARED_RUNTIME_READERS`, one group per matching entry in declaration order, whose reason is
  the class reason followed by the entry's own reason. Selection is unchanged: an embedding
  package and an enclosing package (rule (b)), a literal or glob reader (rule (c), as AFP-V0-152
  narrows it), a declared read scope and an unbounded reader (rule (d)) are selected as before. A
  path a plugin claims as source (`UNINDEXED_SOURCE_PATH`), a `LANGUAGE_FRONTIER` entry (an
  unobserved module, AFP-V0-191, AFP-V0-650) and a path no entry holds keep their entries.
  (3) A classified path is non-source for `participants`, so it admits no plugin and raises no
  path-open frontier of a plugin that neither owns a dirty path, reads any path nor has a reached
  unit (V1-2726). Every reached reader's own frontier still bears under AFP-V0-511 (3) and
  AFP-V0-581: a Go or shell reader that rule (d) selects for every dirty path, or a TypeScript test
  that names `./cmd/corvint` as a build path literal, keeps its plugin's entries.
  (4) A dirty `.corvint/runtime-readers.json` that no unit names or reaches is `CORVINT_DECLARATION`
  (AFP-V0-443, amended to cover both declarations); one a unit names keeps `UNOWNED_DIRTY_PATH`.
  (5) This repository declares `.corvint/changes/` (sealed CEMs, V1-1820), the read-scope and
  unbounded-reader declarations, `cmd/corvint/testdata/`, the use-case ledger and receipts,
  `integrations/compatibility.json`, `internal/capcatalog/catalog.json`,
  `internal/capcatalog/trees/` and `script/line-citation-unpinned-inventory.txt`, each with its
  readers. Genuinely unowned paths stay `UNOWNED_DIRTY_PATH` with the plan `UNKNOWN` and keep the
  AFP-V0-511 path-open frontier (invariant 2; dropping it would widen the Playwright and app-flow
  decisions, AFP-V0-601 (4)): for batch 1028a these are `.github/forward-rows/narrow.jq` and
  `.github/workflows/ci.yml` (CI driver inputs, never declarable) and the opencode package's npm
  manifest and README (`integrations/opencode/package.json`, a TypeScript resolution input, and
  `integrations/opencode/README.md`, which no declaration covers).
  V1-2726, measured on today's graph with every plugin: a documentation-only plan (a spec and a
  build-log entry, both AFP-V0-160 documentation) and PR #1816's path set (80 `LANGUAGE_FRONTIER`
  when filed) each carry 3 entries, `go` 2 and `shell` 1, the frontier of the rule (d) readers every
  plan selects; no plugin without a selected unit contributes one. An undeclared unowned file such as
  `integrations/opencode/README.md` still carries the cfamily, dotnet, ruby and swift path-open
  entries, by AFP-V0-511 (2).
  Measured on batch 1028c with the declaration at base and head: `UNOWNED_DIRTY_PATH` 10 to 0,
  `LANGUAGE_FRONTIER` 86 to 78 (cfamily, dotnet, ruby and swift gone; the 75 typescript entries
  are the frontier of TypeScript tests reached as path-literal readers of the batch's Go and spec
  changes), selected units 182 to 182 with the same set, and the gate selector's output identical;
  the plan stays `UNKNOWN` on those reached readers' frontier alone. Non-goals: any change to the
  gate selector (`internal/gateselect`), which attributes paths by its own rules and does not read
  `nonSource` classes other than task state, to the Playwright declaration check (TJAA-V0-101),
  which reads only `DECLARED_DOCUMENTATION`, and to AFP-V0-511 for paths no declaration holds.
  Failure modes: a declared path that a test of any language does read by a computed name, or
  that a build or CI step consumes, is a false declaration; an excluded unit could then miss an
  edit. The entry's reason names the readers relied on so review can falsify it. Falsifier: a
  dirty declared path whose test outcome changes in a unit the plan excludes. Rollback: delete
  `.corvint/runtime-readers.json` (plans revert byte for byte to AFP-V0-601), or drop the
  `runtimeReaderPaths` step in `selectPlan`.
- **AFP-V0-603:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-3067;
  rule (3) and its `.github` change approved by the owner's `ci-control-plane` OK, 2026-10-28)
  The AFP-V0-098 superset rule compares two selections of one change. The deciding driver
  (`tools/corvint-pr-tests --mode plan`) runs `gateselect.Select`, through
  `tools/gate-affected-select`, over the plan of the `corvint affected` built from
  `CORVINT_PR_TOOL_SOURCE`. The shadow (`corvint test --dry-run`, built from
  `CORVINT_TEST_SHADOW_SOURCE`) narrows `gateselect.SelectStructural` over its own plan. `narrow.jq`
  records `FULL` when the driver's packages are not a superset of the shadow's Go units. Before this
  requirement every batch went FULL on that rule. Measured: PR #1961 (`07f43929f9e9..0d1329160835`)
  had driver 204, shadow 216 and 12 omitted; PR #1957 (`aa3b6e4f0109..ff8b1120af54`) had 356, 389
  and 33. Both reproduce exactly with locally built pins. The shadow pin `5163d1ed` (2026-10-13)
  predates AFP-V0-152 (`8959ecfa4`, 2026-10-21), which the driver pin `0245855002` (2026-10-25)
  carries. All 12 of #1961's omissions are that older shadow's rule (c) readers of the sealed CEM
  (`reader … <- .corvint/changes/ec75c04d….cem.json`), which AFP-V0-152 narrows out. #1957's shadow
  is `FULL` at every version (`UNINDEXED_SOURCE_PATH .github/gate-reuse/find.sh`), so its omission
  is the separate `.github` refusal. Rules:
  (1) At one version the driver covers the shadow: `Select` and `SelectStructural` both start from
  the plan's Go selection (`provider.go.packages`, which holds its `UNBOUNDED_READER` units) and
  apply rules (a) to (c) and the read-scope declarations alike. `Select` only adds its own rule (d)
  closure. A change to either entry point that breaks this inclusion is a defect.
  (2) Replay: PR #1961's non-`.github` paths, through one planner and both entry points on this
  repository, give no package the shadow selects that the driver omits, and `benchmarks/runner`
  (one of the 12 omitted) is selected by neither, so AFP-V0-152 is the recorded rule for its
  absence. With the `resolvedEvidence` narrowing disabled, the replay fails with the CI line.
  (3) Pin rule: `CORVINT_TEST_SHADOW_SOURCE` MUST name a commit that is `CORVINT_PR_TOOL_SOURCE` or
  a descendant of it, so the shadow never runs selection rules older than the driver's. It is set
  equal to `CORVINT_PR_TOOL_SOURCE` (`0245855002c84e45e41e8a63036d48f83df2c4fa`). Replaying PR #1961
  with both tools built from that commit gives driver 204 and shadow 204 (`NARROWED`), with 0
  omitted. V1-3093 re-pinned both to origin/main `b609ca4b266e63ccbf08954edd99a2a914213e47` (the merge of PR #1990), which carries the AFP-V0-800..802 effective-base proof. V1-3309 re-pinned both to `93b9f5b08ce841875c9c81d17905908d91346f99` (the merge of PR #8), because the single-root 1.0 repository does not contain `b609ca4b2`: every pinned checkout failed with "not our ref", no shadow plan was retained and each forward row was INCOMPLETE. `tools/corvint-pr-tests` and `go.mod` are byte-identical at both commits. The forward epoch is not advanced:
  each AFP-V0-047 row records its `toolSource`, and AFP-V0-030 advances the epoch only to re-arm
  after a miss.
  Non-goals: changing `narrow.jq`, the driver or the superset rule, which is correct at one version;
  narrowing a change that touches `.github/` (this one included). Failure modes: a shadow pin newer than the driver pin
  can still select a package a newer planner rule adds, and the superset rule then records FULL; that
  is the safe direction. Falsifier: a non-`.github` PR whose driver and shadow, built from one
  commit, record an omission. Rollback: restore `CORVINT_TEST_SHADOW_SOURCE`
  `5163d1ed4b61084fc32cf79355848899c3e8a5bb` (every batch then records FULL again) and delete
  `replay_v13067_test.go`.
- **AFP-V0-620:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2973)
  A dirty `_test.go` path that no unit lists drops its `UNINDEXED_SOURCE_PATH` entry when all of
  these hold: only the Go plugin claims it; it is absent from the disk checkout the graph was
  observed from, so it was deleted or moved away since the base; no directory the Go plugin
  reports as unobserved (AFP-V0-191) encloses it; and its directory still holds a Go package the
  graph observes. Go compiles a `_test.go` file only into the test binary of the package in its
  own directory, and a directory holds one package, so that package is the file's base package;
  rule (a) of AFP-V0-004 already selects it with a `DIRECT_SOURCE_CHANGE` witness naming the
  path. The entry stays, and the plan stays `UNKNOWN`, for a graph with no disk root (an `fs.FS`
  graph), a path present on disk (a new or build-excluded file whose imports the graph cannot
  see), a deleted path whose directory holds no observed package, and a deleted non-test Go file,
  whose loss can change what other packages compile against beyond the graph's view of it.
  Failure mode: a graph observed from a checkout other than the one the dirty paths describe would
  read absence from the wrong tree; every caller builds the graph from the checkout it diffs.
  Falsifier: a moved or deleted `_test.go` path whose test outcome changes in a package the plan
  excludes. Rollback: drop the `deletedGoTest` check in `seed`; the path raises
  `UNINDEXED_SOURCE_PATH` again.

- **AFP-V0-615:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2952)
  A package whose tests launch a sandbox of their own is never declared in
  `.corvint/test-read-scopes.json`: AFP-V0-023 runs a declared package under the Landlock wrapper,
  which refuses a nested sandbox, so its tests fail confined (bubblewrap under
  `internal/liveverify/mutate`, FPK-V0-142, broke CI on PR #1937). Rules:
  (1) The project owns the list. `.corvint/test-nested-sandboxes.json` is a closed record
  `{"profile":"corvint-test-nested-sandboxes/0","packages":{PACKAGE:REASON}}` mapping each clean
  repository-relative package directory to the sandbox it launches; a missing record is empty, and
  any other shape, an unclean directory or a blank reason exits 2. Nothing is inferred from imports
  or literals: `internal/cem/workflow`, `internal/cemdiscriminate` and
  `internal/liveverify/rbmutate` import or name the sandbox and still pass confined.
  (2) `test-read-scopes-check` (AFP-V0-051) exits 1 for every declared package with tests that
  the record lists, naming the package, the recorded sandbox and the alternative: remove the entry
  and add the package to `units` of `.corvint/unbounded-readers.json` with a reason (AFP-V0-025,
  AFP-V0-033). The `unbounded-readers` unrecorded message names the same case.
  Failure modes: a sandbox-launching package the record omits is still accepted and fails confined
  in CI, as before; the record does not change selection. Falsifier: a declared package the record
  lists for which the check exits 0. Rollback: delete the record and `readSandboxes`; the check
  returns to AFP-V0-051 alone.

- **AFP-V0-640:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2647;
  witness contract decided by the orchestrator: reference, not copy) In the `affected-plan/1`
  document (AFP-V0-035) each `selected` witness MUST replace `via` with `viaLength`, the length of
  the `--full` chain, and MUST state the chain by reference: `plan.viaLinks` maps each unit that
  appears after the first position of some chain to its predecessor, and a selection's chain is
  rebuilt by walking `viaLinks` back `viaLength - 1` steps from its own `unitId`. A link is recorded
  only when the chain ends at its own unit and every prefix it shares with a recorded chain agrees;
  a chain that cannot be stated that way keeps its full `via` and records no link, so the links
  never rebuild a chain the `--full` plan did not carry. `viaLinks` is omitted when empty. The
  `--full` `affected-plan/0` document is unchanged and keeps its copied chains. The receipt then
  grows linearly with chain depth: on the 20,001-file perf-budget Ruby tree (one require chain
  6,668 deep) a warm local call fell from 824,015,703 to 2,386,792 bytes and from 12.1 s to 0.8 s
  (macOS, clean builds; build log `2026-10-28-affected-witness-links`). Limits: the selector still
  copies chains in memory (peak RSS 2.0 GB on that tree; lifted by AFP-V0-641), and the quiet WSL2 measurement of
  AFP-V0-399 is `NOT_RUN`. The profile identifier stays `affected-plan/1` because its CCF freeze
  is still proposed. Falsifier: a selection whose rebuilt chain differs from the `--full` `via`,
  or a compact plan that grows faster than linearly with chain depth. Rollback: restore `Via` in
  the compact witness and drop `viaLinks` and `viaLength` in `cmd/corvint/affected_compact.go`,
  and restore the `via` member in the core-freeze golden `affected-committed-range.json`.
- **AFP-V0-641:** (accepted under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2979;
  amends the AFP-V0-640 memory limit) The selector MUST hold each witness chain by reference: one
  predecessor link per reached unit, shared by every chain that extends it, so selector memory is
  linear in the reached units however deep the chains are. `affected.SelectLinked` returns that plan;
  the default `affected-plan/1` receipt is compacted from it directly, reading chains through
  `Witness.ViaLength`, `Witness.Chain` and `Witness.LastStep`. `affected.Select`, `--full`
  (`affected-plan/0`) and every other caller, including the hashed `--audit-changes` and repository
  guidance receipts, MUST receive copied `via` chains (`Plan.MaterializeVia`), so both wire documents
  stay byte-identical to the copying selector. On the 20,001-file perf-budget Ruby tree (one require
  chain through 6,667 models) a warm default call fell from 1.77-2.12 GB to 116-129 MB peak RSS and from
  0.82-0.84 s to 0.41-0.72 s, with byte-identical default and `--full` output (macOS, clean builds;
  build log `2026-10-28-affected-via-memory`). Limits: `--full` stays quadratic in size and memory
  because its format copies every chain (3.4 GB peak RSS, 824 MB output on that tree), and the quiet
  WSL2 measurement of AFP-V0-399 stays `NOT_RUN`. Falsifier: a default or `--full` output that
  differs from the copying selector's, or selector allocation that grows faster than linearly with
  chain depth. Rollback: make `SelectLinked` callers use `Select` (`affectedSelect` in
  `cmd/corvint/affected.go`); `Select` is unchanged in output.

### Independent nested-module units (AFP-V0-650, V1-2980)

- **AFP-V0-650:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2980)
  A language plugin MAY return `Result.Runnable`: a strictly ascending subset of its
  `Result.Unobserved` directories (AFP-V0-191) that its own runner can test as a root of their
  own. `Build` MUST refuse any other value as `ErrInvalidLanguage`; the graph digest body carries
  them as the `runnable` member (`namespace:directory`), omitted when empty so every earlier
  graph keeps its digest, and the graph cache stores and restores them. The Go plugin reports a
  nested module as runnable only when its AFP-V0-031 evidence closed the frontier or left it open
  only in the dependent direction (AFP-V0-582): no observed module requires or replaces it, its
  `go.mod` was read and parsed, no `go.work` places it, no observed manifest was unreadable
  when it was judged, no other unlisted module's `go.mod` replaces a module with a directory
  inside it (that module compiles its files too), and every unlisted module's `go.mod` was read
  and parsed and its directory replacements resolve inside the repository (amended after
  independent review, 2026-10-28). A dirty path whose longest unobserved directory is runnable MUST be the
  unknown `LANGUAGE_FRONTIER` with detail `<namespace>:unobserved-module:<directory>:<path>` in
  place of the AFP-V0-191 `unobserved-directory` detail. A path no plugin claims there (a README,
  a fixture) MUST carry that entry beside its own `UNOWNED_DIRTY_PATH` entry, so the module still
  runs while the unowned class keeps its own decisions (AFP-V0-601 (4)). The plan stays `UNKNOWN`
  and the gate tier full: AFP-V0-028 and `gateselect` do not admit the new detail, and the
  observed side's selection is unchanged. Only `corvint test` on the local runner profile admits
  it, by adding one execution unit per module that runs the module's own `go test ./...` from its
  directory (STR-V0-064); every other consumer treats it like any unallowed frontier.
  A nested module the root requires or replaces, one whose `go.mod` cannot be read or parsed, one
  in a nested workspace, one another nested module replaces by directory, every nested module
  while some nested `go.mod` is unreadable or has a directory replacement outside the
  repository, and a deeper non-runnable module inside a runnable one keep the
  AFP-V0-191 `unobserved-directory` detail and, through it, fail-closed `FULL` naming the module
  directory.
  Failure modes: a nested module whose tests read the root's files by path is not reached by a
  root change (the AFP-V0-189 non-goal is unchanged), and a `GOWORK` or `GOFLAGS` value the
  plugin cannot observe can make the module build against the root; the STR-V0-064 unit runs
  with `GOWORK=off` for that reason. Non-goals: the CI runner profile and CI shard placement,
  which keep `FULL` until a confined module runner is qualified; indexing the module's packages
  (V1-0872). Falsifier: a nested-module change whose module unit passes while a root package the
  plan left out fails on it. Evidence:
  `TestIndependentNestedModuleIsARunnableModuleFrontier_AFPV0650`,
  `TestNestedModuleTheRootBuildsOrCannotReadIsNotRunnable_AFPV0650`,
  `TestRunnableDirectoriesAreValidatedAndDigested_AFPV0650`, the amended expectations of
  `TestNestedModuleChangeIsANestedModuleUnknown_AFPV0191` and
  `TestIndependentNestedModulesCloseTheFrontier_V1_0867`, and the replay in
  `https://github.com/beamfall/corvint-history/blob/1c4d8793cf71cbb01b8509b68a6cb1502a40a13e/docs/build-log/2026-10-28-nested-module-test-unit.md`. Rollback: drop `Result.Runnable`, the
  digest and cache member, `runnableModules` and `Graph.unobservedModule`; every such path
  returns to the `unobserved-directory` detail and `corvint test` to `FULL`.

- **AFP-V0-670:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2987;
  the `.github` change approved by the owner for building (2026-10-28))
  Exact-tree reuse of a whole gate, shared by AFP-V0-671 and AFP-V0-672. A gate is a fixed set of
  jobs of one workflow, a decision job and a record job; the decision is
  `.github/gate-reuse/find.sh`, run with the gate label, the workflow file name, the exact job names
  (a JSON array, each matrix leg by its listed name) and the record job name. Rules:
  (1) In a `pull_request` run the record job MUST run only when every gated job concluded `success`,
  check out the tested merge commit (depth 2) without credentials, hold no token, run no repository
  code (shell builtins, `git` and `printf` only), check that the commit's second parent is the
  pull-request head, and print one line to its own log:
  `corvint-ci-gate-tree/0 gate=<label> tree=<tree> base=<first parent> head=<second parent> target=<merge commit>`.
  (2) The decision job MUST run only for `merge_group` and `push`, with `actions: read` and
  `contents: read`, as one `continue-on-error` step whose output `mode` is `REUSE` or empty. It MUST
  leave `mode` empty unless the tested commit is a two-parent merge whose `.github` tree equals its
  first parent's: a commit that changes `.github`, which holds every workflow and this script, always
  runs the gate in full.
  (3) For `merge_group`, REUSE only when a successful `pull_request` run of that workflow in this
  repository, whose head is the queue commit's second parent, has exactly one latest job of each
  gated name and exactly one latest record job, all of that run and concluded `success` on that
  head, and that record job's log, read through the job-log API, holds exactly one record line with
  this label whose tree, base and head equal the queue commit's tree, first parent and second
  parent. Equal first parents mean equal committed history, which a gate that reads history (the
  AHI-020 host package version gate) needs as well as an equal tree.
  (4) For `push`, REUSE only on `refs/heads/main` when a successful `merge_group` run of that
  workflow on this exact commit, from this repository and a `gh-readonly-queue/main/` branch, has
  exactly one latest job of each gated name, all of that run and concluded `success` on this commit
  (the AFP-V0-080 rule).
  (5) Every gated job MUST need the decision job, keep running when it is skipped (`!cancelled()`),
  and gate every step on `mode != 'REUSE'`, so on REUSE it concludes `success` with skipped steps
  and the next reuse (rule 4) still sees it passed. No artifact is read or trusted. Any other
  listing, refusal, unreadable response or log, missing or duplicated job or record line, or
  malformed input leaves `mode` empty and the gate runs in full. On REUSE the decision (profile
  `corvint-ci-gate-reuse/0`: gate, mode, event, tree, base, head, target, run id, record job id) is
  written to the step summary.
  Trust argument: as for AFP-V0-163, only the runner executing the record job writes its log, the
  record job runs no repository code, and a pull-request run takes the workflow from the tested
  merge commit, whose tree must equal the queue commit's; rule (2) further refuses any commit whose
  workflows or decision script differ from its first parent's, so a reuse is only ever decided by
  `.github` content that was already on the base and that the `ci-control-plane` admin review
  admitted. Failure modes: a gate whose verdict depends on something outside the tree and history
  (the network, the host image, the date) can pass in the pull-request run and fail on a rerun; the
  gated jobs run with `GOPROXY=off` on pinned actions, and that residual is accepted as for
  AFP-V0-163. A record line of a pull-request run that a later force-push superseded still names its
  own head, which a queue entry uses only when that head is its second parent. Falsifier: a queue
  entry or main push that decided REUSE for a tree, base or head no passed run recorded, or for a
  commit that changes `.github`. Rollback: delete the decision and record jobs and the step and job
  conditions they added; every gate runs in full on every event.
- **AFP-V0-671:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2987)
  The `doc-gates` job of `.github/workflows/ci.yml` is an AFP-V0-670 gate labelled `doc-gates`,
  decided by `doc-gates-reuse` and recorded by `doc-gates-record`. It keeps running for a
  `ci:batched` constituent (AFP-V0-081) and its `make`/`go run` commands are unchanged, so the
  `script/batch-precheck.sh` twin (ASD-V0-036) still mirrors exactly those commands; the decision
  and record commands live in their own jobs, which the twin never reads.
  Failure modes: the decision job adds about 10 s before `doc-gates` on a queue entry or main push;
  a pull request pays only the record job, which never fails CI. Falsifier: a REUSE `doc-gates`
  whose host package version history (AHI-020) differs from the run that passed. Rollback: as
  AFP-V0-670 for this job only.
- **AFP-V0-672:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2987)
  The CEM release gate of `.github/workflows/cem-release-gate.yml` (AFP-V0-432) is an AFP-V0-670
  gate labelled `cem-release`, decided by `cem-release-reuse` and recorded by
  `cem-release-record`; its gated jobs are both `cem-release-gate` matrix legs (`cem-release-gate
  (ubuntu-24.04)`, `cem-release-gate (macos-15)`) and `cem-release-readiness`, which now also needs
  the decision job and runs only after the gate concluded `success`. The record job needs both
  gated jobs to have concluded `success`. The `ci:batched` conditions and the strict outcome of
  every release path are unchanged. On REUSE no run record, outcome or retained artifact is
  produced for that event; the pull-request run of the same tree and base holds them.
  Failure modes: the suites read only committed objects of HEAD (CEM-V1-572), so an equal tree is an
  equal suite verdict, but a host image change between the pull-request run and the queue entry is
  not observed again. Falsifier: a CEM release gate failure on a rerun of a REUSE tree on the same
  pinned runners. Rollback: as AFP-V0-670 for this workflow only.

### Merge-queue narrowing and narrowed-run reuse (AFP-V0-730 to AFP-V0-735; V1-3064)

Intent (owner decision 2026-10-28, V1-3064): the orchestrator moves from batch pull requests to
one pull request per lane through a grouped merge queue, once lane pull requests narrow
(V1-2941). Until now every queue entry ran the full eight-shard suite (about 30 minutes, measured on
#1957 before V1-3051): `AFP-V0-026` kept every pull-request-gated narrowing off for `merge_group`,
and `AFP-V0-046` reused only a pull-request run that tested the entry's tree in full, which a
narrowed lane pull request never is and a grouped entry stacked on earlier entries never matches.
A queue entry now selects over its whole queue range under the same narrowing verdict as pull
requests, and the first entry of a group may reuse a narrowed pull-request run of the same range.
The main push of a narrowed or narrowed-reusing entry runs the full suite, so the full-suite
safety net moves from before the merge to just after it. Every missing input is FULL.

Non-goals: no change to how a pull request selects or narrows; no new planner, selector or pin
(the queue uses the `CORVINT_PR_TOOL_SOURCE` planner and selector and the
`CORVINT_TEST_SHADOW_SOURCE` shadow); no change to repository rulesets or to the queue's grouping
settings, which stay the owner's; no narrowing of `push`, release or non-race jobs.

- **AFP-V0-730:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept, 2026-10-28; V1-3064;
  amends AFP-V0-026, AFP-V0-097) The `narrowing-verdict` job SHALL also run for a `merge_group`
  event whose `base_ref` is `refs/heads/main`, only while the repository variable
  `CORVINT_MQ_NARROWING` is `on` (unset by default, separate from `CORVINT_PR_NARROWING` so the queue
  is enabled and rolled back on its own). It checks out the event's `base_sha` (a commit of
  `main`, never the entry) and runs the same `accept.sh`, so a queue entry is admitted by exactly
  the AFP-V0-097 verdict a pull request is. A skipped job, a job failure, an unreadable listing or
  any verdict other than `MET` leaves the entry FULL.
- **AFP-V0-731:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept, 2026-10-28; V1-3064)
  Under a `MET` verdict, while `CORVINT_MQ_NARROWING` is `on` and docs-plan selected neither DOCS
  nor REUSE, the `go-product-shard` narrowing gate of a `merge_group` entry on `main` SHALL extract
  `.github/forward-rows` from the queue base with `git archive` and run its `mq-narrow.sh` in the
  tested checkout. The selection range is `base_sha .. github.sha`, the whole queue range, so an
  entry stacked on earlier entries of its group also selects for their changes. `mq-narrow.sh`
  requires `base_ref` `refs/heads/main`, a `head_sha` equal to the tested commit, a clean checkout
  of it, and `base_sha` an ancestor of both the tested commit and `refs/remotes/origin/main`. It runs
  the pinned `corvint affected --base base_sha`, admits only an `affected-plan/0|1` receipt that is
  `ok`, non-mutating and names this revision and base, runs the pinned
  `gate-affected-select` on it, and admits only a `run <packages>` verdict whose every package is
  the root module or under it; it then re-checks the tree. It writes that verdict as a
  driver-shaped selection (`corvint-mq-selection/0`) and hands it to `narrow.sh` (`SELECTION`), which
  applies every `AFP-V0-098` and `AFP-V0-590` guard unchanged: no `.github/` path in the range, the
  pinned shadow `corvint test --dry-run` over the same base is a subset, and the balanced placement
  is recomputable. A `FALLBACK` verdict (the selector's answer to an UNKNOWN plan), a `NOTHING`
  verdict, a planner or selector failure, any mismatch above, or any `narrow.sh` refusal records
  FULL and runs the whole shard; the script never fails the shard.
- **AFP-V0-732:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept, 2026-10-28; V1-3064;
  amends AFP-V0-098, AFP-V0-591) The tests step SHALL narrow a `merge_group` shard from a retained
  `NARROWED` record exactly as a pull-request shard (same record, sidecar, placement and fallbacks).
  A `merge_group` shard SHALL upload its coverage marker, and `go-product` SHALL apply the
  AFP-V0-591 guard to a `merge_group` run as to a pull request, so a queue entry passes only when
  every shard ran its partition or every shard ran its part of one balanced placement.
- **AFP-V0-733:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept, 2026-10-28; V1-3064;
  amends AFP-V0-099) A narrowed queue entry SHALL write no `AFP-V0-170` tested-tree record (the
  existing every-shard-`FULL` condition under a `MET` verdict), so the `main` push of its commit
  finds nothing to reuse and runs the full suite. The forward-row collector (`collect.sh`) SHALL
  write no row for a queue run whose own `ci-narrowing-*` records include a `NARROWED` one, or whose
  `ci-reuse` decision is a `REUSE` of scope `NARROWED`: such a run holds no full outcomes, so it is
  neither CLEAN nor an INCOMPLETE row that would count toward the AFP-V0-030 kill switch.
  Undownloadable records leave the row INCOMPLETE. A queue run whose own gate recorded only FULL is
  a row as before.
- **AFP-V0-734:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept, 2026-10-28; V1-3064;
  amends AFP-V0-046, AFP-V0-170) A new `ci-narrowed-tree-record` job SHALL, in a pull-request run
  only, after `go-product` and every shard succeeded under a `MET` verdict with at least one shard
  not `FULL` and docs-plan neither DOCS nor REUSE, print one line
  `corvint-ci-tested-tree/2 scope=NARROWED tree=TREE target=COMMIT base=BASE head=HEAD shards=N`
  naming the tested merge, its first parent and its second parent (the pull-request head); it
  prints nothing unless that first parent equals the event's `pull_request.base.sha`, the base the
  shards selected over, since a recomputed merge ref can sit on another commit. It runs no repository code, as the AFP-V0-170 record job.
  `tools/ci-reuse-plan` SHALL accept such a line, only from that job's log, only for
  `--event merge_group` with `--base` the queue commit's first parent, and only when tree, head,
  base and shard count all match; a full AFP-V0-170 record of the same run is preferred, and a main
  push never accepts a narrowed record. The decision then carries `scope: "NARROWED"` and `base`.
  Equal tree and equal first parent make the pull-request merge and the entry the same range, so
  the selection proven by AFP-V0-591 in the pull-request run is the selection the entry would make.
  The docs-plan reuse step SHALL read that job's log only for a merge-queue entry and pass
  `--base` only to a tool that accepts it, so a tool built from a `main` that predates it keeps the
  AFP-V0-046 full-record reuse. A REUSE entry writes no tested-tree record, so its main push runs in full.
- **AFP-V0-735:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept, 2026-10-28; V1-3064)
  Promotion needs measured queue-entry durations of at least two grouped entries with
  `CORVINT_MQ_NARROWING` on, recorded in `https://github.com/beamfall/corvint-history/blob/1c4d8793cf71cbb01b8509b68a6cb1502a40a13e/docs/build-log/2026-10-28-merge-queue-narrowing.md` by its
  procedure, beside the full-suite baseline. Until then the hosted behavior is `NOT_OBSERVED`.

Failure modes: (1) a narrowing miss in a queue entry is caught only by the full `main` push after
the merge, not before it; the push fails on `main` and the owner reverts. (2) Queue rows stop
feeding the AFP-V0-029 bar for narrowed entries (AFP-V0-733), so with the queue narrowed the bar's
window refills only from FULL entries; a starved window lets the verdict lapse after seven days,
which turns pull-request and queue narrowing off together (fail-closed, not silent). (3) A grouped
entry's range includes earlier entries' changes, so it selects at least the union of their
selections; a group that touches `.github/` anywhere runs FULL. (4) A REUSE of a narrowed pull-request
run trusts that run's pinned tools; a pin bump is a `.github/` change and so a FULL run. (5) The
first queue run of this change builds `ci-reuse-plan` from the previous `main`, which lacks
`--base`, so it is not passed and only full-record reuse applies; the base `.github/forward-rows`
lacks `mq-narrow.sh` until merged, so its gate fails closed to FULL. (6) A `merge_group` run uses
the workflow file of the queue head, so the gate step itself is entry-controlled: as for pull
requests, only the ci-control-plane admin review of `ci.yml` holds that boundary, and a `.github/`
change makes the range FULL.

Falsifier: a `main` push of a commit whose queue entry narrowed (or reused a narrowed pull-request
run) fails a package that the entry's selection omitted while every shard of the entry passed.
That is a narrowing MISS of the queue path; the owner turns `CORVINT_MQ_NARROWING` off.

Rollback: unset `CORVINT_MQ_NARROWING` (immediate; the entry runs as before this change, except that
AFP-V0-734 reuse still applies). Full rollback reverts the `merge_group` branches of the
`narrowing-verdict` condition, the gate, the tests-step filter, the marker steps and the
`go-product` guard, removes `ci-narrowed-tree-record` and the reuse step's `--base`, and removes
`mq-narrow.sh`, the `SELECTION` input of `narrow.sh`, the `collect.sh` exclusions and the
narrowed-record path of `tools/ci-reuse-plan`.

### Merge-queue reuse of a narrowed record whose base moved (AFP-V0-812 to AFP-V0-816; V1-3100)

Intent (orchestrator lane request, 2026-10-28; V1-3100): AFP-V0-734 reuses a narrowed
pull-request run only for an equal tree and first parent. Once `main` moves after the pull-request
run, the entry falls back to its whole AFP-V0-731 queue-range selection: PR #1991's narrowed run
(run 40803231280) was followed by a `merge_group` run (40807179829) that ran 296 packages because
`main` had moved to `b609ca4b2`. When only the first parent moved, the packages the main delta
cannot have changed were already tested by the record, so the entry SHALL run only the rest and
record the reuse.

Notation: TM is the queue entry, Q its first parent (the queue base), H its second parent, and the
record names tree P, base R and head H. C is a commit of tree P with parents R and H. `sel(X)` is
the AFP-V0-731 selection of the range X..TM. Non-goals: a full (non-narrowed) record of a moved
base, a stacked entry (Q is not on `main`), a FULL queue range, and any change to AFP-V0-734
exact reuse.

- **AFP-V0-812:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept, 2026-10-11; V1-3100;
  amends AFP-V0-734) When `tools/ci-reuse-plan --event merge_group --base Q` decides FULL and an
  admitted pull-request run's `ci-narrowed-tree-record` job log holds exactly one narrowed line
  whose head equals the entry's head and whose shard count matches, but whose base or tree is not
  the entry's, the decision SHALL carry `delta: {runId, recordJobId, tree, target, base, head}` of
  the first such run in the existing admission order. Exact reuse is unchanged and carries no
  `delta`; a push or a decision without `--base` never carries one. The docs-plan reuse step SHALL
  hand only a FULL `corvint-ci-reuse/0` decision's `delta`, whose head equals the queue head's
  second parent and whose fields are ids and full commit ids, to the narrowing gate as `MQ_DELTA`.
  The record is still read only from that job's log, as AFP-V0-734 requires.
- **AFP-V0-813:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept, 2026-10-11; V1-3100;
  amends AFP-V0-731) `mq-narrow.sh`, from the queue base's scripts and pinned tools, SHALL accept
  `MQ_DELTA` only when: the entry's parents are exactly Q then H; R is an ancestor of Q;
  `git merge-tree --write-tree R H` equals P; C rebuilt with a fixed identity and date is a commit;
  neither R..TM nor C..TM touches `.github/`, decided by the exit status of
  `git diff --quiet --no-renames B TM -- .github/` (0 no change, 1 a change, any other status
  unreadable) because `--name-only` quotes a non-ASCII path; and the pinned planner and selector
  give a `run` or `NOTHING` verdict over both C..TM (the main delta) and R..TM (the record's
  range), each receipt naming its own base and carrying `.plan.scope` `BOUNDED`, whatever the
  selector prints. C is an unreferenced object; the checkout and refs are unchanged.
- **AFP-V0-814:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept, 2026-10-11; V1-3100;
  amends AFP-V0-098) The selection document handed to `narrow.sh` MAY carry `Reused`, a package
  list disjoint from `Packages`, with `Reuse`, an applied record naming the record run and job ids,
  R and Q as full commit ids and `reused` equal to the list's length. `narrow.jq` SHALL count
  `Reused` toward the AFP-V0-098 shadow superset and record it as `reused` beside `reuse`, not in
  `skipped`, and SHALL be FULL for a reused list that is not a package list, that names no such
  applied record, or that overlaps the selection. The record also carries `reuse` (applied or not,
  with its reason, both bases, `selected`, `deltaSelected`, `recordSelected`, `intersection` and
  `reused` counts), and the shard summary names the reused count and record run.
- **AFP-V0-815:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept, 2026-10-11; V1-3100)
  With S = sel(Q), D = sel(C) and Rs = sel(R), the entry SHALL reuse exactly (S ∩ Rs) \ D and run
  `Packages` = S minus that set, which `intersection` counts. A package in Rs passed in the record
  run against tree P; one not in D is not reached by any path that differs between P and TM. The
  run set therefore holds every queue-range package the record did not run (S \ Rs) and every one
  the main delta reaches (S ∩ D), so no selected package goes unrun without a recorded reuse. The
  literal "PR range ∩ main delta" would use R..Q, which misses dependency edges that H itself adds.
  Every shard computes the same sets, so AFP-V0-590 placement and the AFP-V0-591 identical
  `selected` guard hold over the run set.
- **AFP-V0-816:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept, 2026-10-11; V1-3100)
  When any AFP-V0-813 check fails (no or a malformed `MQ_DELTA`, other parents, a base that is not
  an ancestor, a tree mismatch, a `.github/` change or an unreadable `.github/` diff, a FALLBACK
  verdict, a receipt whose `.plan.scope` is not `BOUNDED` (UNKNOWN even under a `run` verdict), or
  a planner or selector failure on either range), the entry SHALL keep the whole AFP-V0-731 selection, `reused`
  SHALL be empty and `reuse` SHALL be `{applied: false, reason}`, which the shard summary prints.
  The docs-plan help probe SHALL read the tool's help text whole before matching `-base`, because
  `grep -q` exiting at its first match made the tool's next write raise SIGPIPE, which `pipefail`
  turned into a failed probe, so `--base` was never passed and AFP-V0-734 never applied.

Failure modes: (1) a reused package that the delta's plan wrongly omits is caught only by the main
push after the merge, as for AFP-V0-731; D is the pinned planner's selection, so its UNKNOWN or
FALLBACK refuses reuse. (2) The record run's pass is trusted as AFP-V0-734 trusts it, through its
pinned tools; a pin bump is a `.github/` change, which refuses reuse. (3) Three plans per entry
cost about three planner runs on each shard. (4) The first queue run after this change uses the
queue base's scripts, which lack `MQ_DELTA`, so it reuses nothing (fail-closed).
