Falsifier: a main push fails a package that an entry recorded as reused while every shard of the
entry and of the record run passed.

Rollback: unset `CORVINT_MQ_NARROWING` (immediate, as for AFP-V0-731). Full rollback removes the
`delta` member and `narrowedRecordOf` from `tools/ci-reuse-plan`, the `mqDelta` output and
`MQ_DELTA` env from `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `reuse_` and `plan_` prefixes from
`mq-narrow.sh`, and `Reused`, `reused` and `reuse` from `narrow.jq` and `narrow.sh`; the help-probe
fix is independent and may stay.

### Artifact listing pages read as smaller pages (AFP-V0-760; V1-3084)

Intent (orchestrator lane request, 2026-10-28; V1-3084): an uncached page of 100 of
`repos/$REPO/actions/artifacts` can come back truncated ("unexpected end of JSON input") after about
8 s on every try, while the same artifacts read cleanly as pages of 25. `list_names` of
`.github/forward-rows/names.sh` read only pages of 100, so one such page put a named limit on the
AFP-V0-097 listing and the narrowing verdict went FULL.

- **AFP-V0-760:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept, 2026-10-11; V1-3084;
  amends the listing of AFP-V0-047 and AFP-V0-097) `list_names PAGES OUT [TIMES]` SHALL read each page
  of 100 once; when that read fails it SHALL read the same artifacts as the page's four pages of 25
  (page P of 100 is pages 4P-3 to 4P of 25), each with up to three tries 5 s and 10 s apart. PAGES,
  `pages_read` and `exhausted` keep their units and meaning: the callers' budgets (bar.sh 20,
  verdict.sh 400, accept.sh 25 pages of 100) cover the same newest artifacts, a short page of 100 or
  of 25 ends the listing with `exhausted=1`, and `pages_read` counts whole pages of 100. A page of 25
  that fails all three tries SHALL set `limit` to a reason naming the page of 100, the page of 25 and
  the last error, leave `pages_read` at the last whole page and `exhausted` empty, and return; the
  callers then report the named limit and run FULL, as for any unreadable page.

Non-goals: reading every page as pages of 25 (four times the requests against the about 1000 per
hour `GITHUB_TOKEN` budget, about 945 reads for the 400-page verdict), retrying a page of 100 (a
truncated page of 100 was observed to fail on every try), and any change to which artifacts count.
Failure modes: (1) a page of 25 that is also unreadable still stops the listing with a named limit
and FULL; (2) the fallback costs up to four reads and 15 s of waits for each failed page of 100, so
a fully truncated 400-page listing could take far longer than before, bounded by the job timeout,
whose expiry also leaves FULL; (3) artifacts uploaded between the failed read and its pages of 25
shift the listing, as between any two pages before this change. Acceptance evidence: the tests in
the traceability row and the live measurement in
`https://github.com/beamfall/corvint-history/blob/1c4d8793cf71cbb01b8509b68a6cb1502a40a13e/docs/build-log/2026-10-28-v1-3084-artifact-page-split.md`; the hosted verdict is `NOT_OBSERVED`
until the change reaches `main`, where `accept.sh` runs from the base commit. Rollback: revert the
change to `names.sh`; the listing reads pages of 100 only, as before.

### Temp-root-only literals are no repository read (AFP-V0-828; V1-3137)

Intent (orchestrator lane request, 2026-10-29; V1-3137): `internal/doccorpus` tests build subject
IDs such as `"native:deployable:.github/workflows/" + file` for fixture repositories written under
`t.TempDir()`, and `tools/test-read-scopes-check` resolved the `.github/workflows/` token from the
repository root because another test of the package climbs to it, so the lane had to record a
literal that no test reads.

- **AFP-V0-828:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept, 2026-10-11; V1-3137;
  amends the AFP-V0-023 check) The check SHALL NOT resolve from the repository root an AFP-V0-021
  token of a test file whose every literal occurrence in that file lies in a temp-root-only
  function; the token SHALL still resolve against the package directory. A top-level function is
  temp-root-only when it, or a function literal inside it, calls `TempDir` on a parameter of type
  `*testing.T`, `*testing.B`, `*testing.F` or `testing.TB` that the function never reassigns, or
  calls `os.MkdirTemp`, and neither it nor any same-package test-file function, method or
  package-level variable it names, transitively, (a) holds a root-locating literal (a parent-only
  token, the Git root lookup, `GOMOD` or `GOWORK`), (b) calls `runtime.Caller`, `runtime.Callers`,
  `os.Getwd`, `os.Executable` or `filepath.Abs`, (c) selects from a non-standard import or sits
  in a file with any dot import, standard or not, (d) selects through a name it declares locally
  that also names an import (a shadowed import is not the import; a shadowed `os` also creates no
  temporary root through `MkdirTemp`), or (e) selects through a qualifier that is neither an import
  name, a name it declares, nor a same-package test-file declaration, such as an import whose
  package name its path does not spell (`gopkg.in/yaml.v3` as `yaml`). A literal outside every
  function (a package-level constant or variable) is never exempt, and no token of a package whose
  tests call `Chdir` anywhere is exempt. Names are matched without scope resolution, so shadowing
  can only withhold the exemption.

Non-goals: no data-flow proof that a literal reaches only the temporary root, no reading of
non-test package code or of other packages, and no change to join runs (V1-2117), `go list`
dependencies (V1-2037) or the recorded-literal file. Failure modes: (1) a test that reaches the
root through a route the rule does not list (an environment variable naming the checkout, a
standard call returning it) and spells the token only in a temp-rooting function is not flagged;
the confined CI shard still denies that read, so the failure is a later and less specific error,
not a silent pass; (2) a conservative refusal (a helper from another package, a shared name) keeps
a literal flagged, which a recorded literal still resolves. Acceptance evidence: the tests in the
traceability row, which fail the four acceptance cases without the rule; on the
`lanes/gh2006-2009` tree the check reports its `.github/workflows` record stale. Rollback: delete
`tools/test-read-scopes-check/temp_root.go` and the `exempt` term in `check`.

### Escaped closing tags and build-constrained test files (AFP-V0-840, AFP-V0-841; V1-3207, V1-3201)

Intent (orchestrator lane request, 2026-10-30; V1-3207, V1-3201): two reports of `corvint affected`
over-selecting. Adding `script/core-n1-source.sh` selected 90 units including
`internal/analyzerhtmlcss` and `internal/jstestprovider`, and changing only the `//go:build` line of
`internal/doccompiler/draft_fifo_unix_test.go` looked like an unbounded fan-out. Reproduced against
`b469ab710` with a binary built from that tree, the first selects 49 units (43 unbounded readers
under AFP-V0-012 rule (d), which every dirty path reaches, and six path-literal readers); the 90
and the analyzer packages came from an installed binary built before the current lexicon. The one
literal false positive left was `internal/console`, whose `"&lt;/script&gt;"` lexed to `/script`.
The second does not reproduce: the build-tag edit selects the same 56 units as a comment-only edit
of the same file, dirty and with `--base`.

- **AFP-V0-840:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept, 2026-10-11; V1-3207;
  amends AFP-V0-505) In the Go plugin's AFP-V0-021 lexicon, a `/` directly after the HTML entity
  `&lt;`, in any letter case, closes an escaped markup tag as a `/` after `<` does: the token
  starts after it, so `&lt;/script&gt;` yields the lone name `script`, which names only a file
  called `script` and never the directory `script/`. A path literal still selects a reader only
  when it names the changed path or a directory that holds it: a bare or prefixed file name
  (`source.sh`, `foo-source.sh`) never selects `script/core-n1-source.sh`, while
  `../script/core-n1-source.sh` and `../script/` do. Proof that the dropped units cannot observe the
  change: an HTML entity is escaped markup text, not a path a test opens. Failure mode: a literal
  that really spells a root path right after `&lt;` loses its anchor and is observed only through
  rule (a), a declared read scope or rule (d). The other plugins keep the wider rule and
  over-select only. Falsifier: a unit whose only link to a dirty path is `&lt;/name` in one
  literal and whose test reads that path. Rollback: remove `closesEscapedTag`.
- **AFP-V0-841:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept, 2026-10-11; V1-3201)
  A dirty build-constrained Go test file, including one whose only change is its `//go:build`
  line, SHALL select its owning package's tests by `DIRECT_TEST_CHANGE` as any test-file change
  does, and no other package through it: no package imports a test file, and the constraint
  changes only which platforms compile it. The platforms the plan did not build stay the retained
  `go:build-constraint-variants` unknown, so the scope stays `UNKNOWN`; selection is by path, so
  the constraint's content never widens it. Units that rule (d) or another witness selects for
  every dirty path are still selected, as for any other change. Failure mode: a test that reads a
  build-constrained test file's bytes is found only through its own literal, read scope or rule
  (d). Rollback: none needed; this pins existing behaviour.

Non-goals: no change to rule (d), whose unbounded readers dominate both plans, and no
per-platform plan. Acceptance evidence: `TestPathLiteralBoundary_AFPV0840`, which fails without
`closesEscapedTag` (the escaped package is selected), and
`TestBuildConstrainedTestFileSelectsOwner_AFPV0841`; the build log entry
`docs/build-log/2026-10-30-affected-literal-boundary-and-build-tags.md` records the repository
reproductions.
### Go builds of repository source are dependency reads (AFP-V0-847; V1-3210)

Intent (orchestrator lane request, 2026-10-30; V1-3210): at d92b0a2a5 `tools/compat-trial` tests
built the tool and its fixtures with `procgroup.Spec{Argv: []string{goPath, "build", ..., "."}}`
and `"./fixtures/" + variant`, and the tool newly imported `internal/archivepath`; the check
covered only `go list` commands (AFP-V0-051 (8)), so it passed and the confined CI shard denied
the read (fixed by de68fce5f).

- **AFP-V0-847:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept, 2026-10-11; V1-3210;
  extends AFP-V0-051 (8)) When a test file of a declared package spells a go command as the
  string-slice `Argv` of a `procgroup.Spec` literal or as the arguments of `exec.Command` or
  `exec.CommandContext`, whose go binary is a `"go"` literal or an identifier or selected field
  whose name contains `go` in any case (such as `goPath`), directly followed by a literal verb
  `build`, `run`, `vet`, `test` or `install`, the check SHALL list its targets with `go list -deps`
  and exit 1, naming the package, the test file and the directory, for every repository directory
  of a non-standard package they load that is not the package directory, below it, or under a
  declared directory entry. A target is a literal `.` or one starting with `./` or `../`, resolved
  as in AFP-V0-051 (8); a `+` concatenation whose leftmost literal starts so stands for the
  `/...` pattern of that literal's directory (`"./fixtures/" + variant` is `./fixtures/...`); the
  separate value of a value-taking flag (`-o`, `-tags`, `-ldflags`, `-run`, `-count` and the
  other go build and test flags that take one) is never one; a command with no positional
  argument, only flags, builds `.`. The targets of `test` and `vet` are listed with
  `go list -deps -test`, so the imports of their test files count too.
  A directory at or below a path the package records in `.corvint/test-read-scope-literals.json`
  is not reported and keeps that record in use, for a build the confined shard never runs.

Non-goals: a target held in a variable, a file name such as `main.go`, an import path, a command
built through any other helper, or a string slice that is no `procgroup.Spec` `Argv` (expected
plans and argv data that tests compare) is not seen; the Landlock wrapper stays the enforcement.
Failure modes: (1) a missed build shape fails later in the confined shard, as before; (2) a build
that runs only off the confined shard (a darwin-only or opt-in live test) is reported until its
directories are declared or recorded. Acceptance evidence: the test in the traceability row; the
check run against d92b0a2a5 exits 1 naming `tools/compat-trial` and `internal/archivepath`, and the
current tree, with the `conformance/go-live-test-v0` darwin-only provider build's directories
declared, exits 0. Rollback: delete `goBuildTargets`, `specArgv`, `goBinary`, `buildTarget`,
`recordedAbove` and the `built` finding, and the `/...` suffix handling in `dependencyDirectories`.

### A root-resolved refusal names its locating literal (AFP-V0-829; V1-3234)

Intent (orchestrator lane request, 2026-10-30; V1-3234): one test-file literal that locates the
repository root makes every token of the package root-relative, withholding the AFP-V0-828
exemption from any token used outside a temp-root-only function, and the refusal named neither
that literal nor the rule, so a lane could not tell why a fixture path counted as a read.

- **AFP-V0-829:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept, 2026-10-30; V1-3234;
  amends the AFP-V0-023 refusal) When a refused repository path is reached only by resolving the
  token from the repository root (not from the package directory), the refusal SHALL also name the
  first root-locating literal of the package's test files as `FILE:LINE holds LITERAL` and state
  the rule: such a literal in any test file makes the package's tokens root-relative (AFP-V0-021),
  and only a token whose every literal lies in a temp-root-only test function is exempt
  (AFP-V0-828). A path the package directory resolves names no locating literal. Non-goals: naming
  every locating literal, or which AFP-V0-828 condition withheld a function's exemption. Failure
  mode: a root-locating token the lexicon reads outside any string literal is named by the line of
  its first textual occurrence. Rollback: drop `rootedBy` and `rootLocator`; the refusal loses the
  clause only.
### Frame metadata and embedded JavaScript (V1-2758, V1-2898)

- `AFP-V0-810`: (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2758)
  `runtime.Caller`, `runtime.Callers`, `runtime.CallersFrames` and `runtime.FuncForPC` SHALL
  never be file-read or root-locator witnesses, including aliased and dot imports and packages
  importing filesystem-capable or third-party libraries. They read frame metadata, not files.
  This supersedes AFP-V0-466. Other root-locator, literal-reader, declared-scope and dependency
  rules remain unchanged; `internal/gateselect` applies the same exclusion, so its rule (d)
  set stays equal to the planner's (AFP-V0-464).
  A navidrome-like logger fixture SHALL select only the edited persistence package and its
  dependents. The p06 replay acceptance is fewer than half its Go packages. This does not
  claim that a downstream consumer cannot use a frame filename to open a file; such a read
  needs its own evidence, and arbitrary data-flow analysis is outside this change.
- `AFP-V0-811`: (accepted under the owner's standing auto-accept (2026-10-11); V1-2898)
  A JavaScript asset embedded by Go SHALL still select its reading Go package under rule (b).
  An embed alone SHALL NOT suppress `NO_SELECTABLE_TEST` for the JavaScript unit: copying
  bytes into a Go binary establishes neither JavaScript test membership nor execution.
  Preserve the language-qualified unknown until the graph has a selectable test dependency
  for that unit. Go reader selection and JavaScript test uncertainty are separate claims.
  Failure mode: treating a Go test that merely checks nonempty embedded bytes as proof of
  JavaScript behavior would falsely close the language's frontier. No wire change is needed;
  rollback is reverting the corresponding code, tests and these clauses.

### A stale self-binary is a degradation (AFP-V0-846; V1-3216)

Intent (orchestrator lane request, 2026-10-30; V1-3216): a `corvint` binary installed on
2026-10-27 planned 90 units on a WSL lane where current source plans 49, and two tickets were
filed from its plan. Nothing in the receipt said the binary was older than the source it planned.

- **AFP-V0-846:** (experimental under the owner's standing lane auto-accept, 2026-10-11; V1-3216)
  When the binary carries a Go build stamp naming a Git commit (`vcs.revision`) and its main module
  path equals the module path of the root's `go.mod`, Corvint SHALL compare that commit with
  `HEAD`: equal commits are fresh after one `rev-parse`; otherwise one tree diff, limited to `*.go`
  files that are not `_test.go` and not under `testdata/`, plus `go.mod` and `go.sum`, decides.
  When it names at least one path the binary is `self-binary-stale`; when the comparison cannot
  complete (the commit is absent here, Git fails, or the shared 2-second deadline expires) it is
  `self-binary-unverified`, never stale. For either code, `corvint affected` SHALL add the
  top-level member `"degradations": ["<code>"]` to its receipt (compact and `--full`) and print one
  stderr line `corvint: DEGRADED <code>: <detail>`, and `corvint test` SHALL print
  `corvint-test: DEGRADED <code>: <detail>` on stderr. The detail names the stamped commit's first
  12 hex digits, for a stale binary the count of differing paths (`at least N` when the 64 KiB
  output bound truncated the list, whose possibly partial last path is dropped) and at most three
  of them, and the recovery `go install ./cmd/corvint`. The member is omitted otherwise. The plan,
  the selection, the exit status and every other member are unchanged. Cost: one in-memory
  build-info read and, only in the binary's own repository, one hermetic `rev-parse` and, for a
  binary not built at `HEAD`, one `git diff --name-only` of two commits; no network and no write.
  Non-goals: comparing uncommitted worktree edits with the binary, or judging a binary built from
  a modified worktree (`vcs.modified=true`) by anything but its stamped commit; checking a binary
  with no Git stamp, such as a release archive or the dogfood wrappers' `-buildvcs=false` builds,
  which report nothing; refusing the plan. Failure modes: (1) a change confined to a non-Go input
  the binary embeds (a `go:embed` asset) is not seen; (2) any Go file outside `cmd/corvint`'s
  build, including a nested module such as `interop/cem01-go`, still counts, so a binary may be
  reported stale for a change that does not alter it, which the recovery resolves. Acceptance
  evidence: `TestSelfBinaryStaleComparesStampedCommitWithHEAD`,
  `TestSelfBinaryDiffPathsTruncation` and `TestSelfBinaryStaleIsReportedByAffectedAndTest`; live, a
  binary built at `3386ab26e327` run in a checkout of `a08b9815f31a` reported 16 differing Go paths
  in both outputs. Rollback: delete `cmd/corvint/self_binary.go` and its three call sites, and the
  `Degradations` members.

## Non-goals and authority

No provider modification; execution only through the explicitly admitted AFP-V0-013 driver; no watcher or daemon (invariant 7,
`GPK-V0-010`); no exclusion certificates as authority; no non-Go provider package lists; no
LPCV-V0 requirement is implemented or promoted by this slice. The fast tier (AFP-V0-011) is not the
push or release gate and MUST NOT replace `make gate` in any declaration until a 200-commit shadow
run shows no selected-set miss the plan did not mark `UNKNOWN`; a narrowed run proves nothing about
the packages it omitted (AFP-V0-004). The AFP-V0-040 cost-drift report is advisory only: no
required check, commit status, automatic table refresh or commit, and no effect on shard
membership or placement.

AFP-V0-382 to AFP-V0-388 do not read any variable the caller did not name, do not model
`NODE_OPTIONS` preloads, shell wrappers, `globalSetup` or other process.env writers (each keeps
the unknown), and do not resolve the TJAA-V0-047 e2e-runtime-dependency frontier. AFP-V0-405 to
AFP-V0-408 model only the dotenv prelude loads they name: no `dotenv/config`, `dotenv-expand`,
`@dotenvx`, `process.loadEnvFile`, variable expansion, multiline or escaped values, encrypted
`.vault` files or a runtime `DOTENV_KEY`/`DOTENV_CONFIG_*` setting. AFP-V0-409 to AFP-V0-413 do not
evaluate computed patterns, titles or tags, run Playwright, or read tags through a package outside
the repository.
AFP-V0-389/404 do not change the `--playwright-config` mode or alias resolution for another
runner's loader.
AFP-V0-420 to AFP-V0-422 move no job other than `go-product-shard`, never run a fork pull request,
a push or `pull_request_target` on the self-hosted runner, never let it post a commit status, and
never provision the host, and do not isolate jobs from one another on a persistent host;
registering the runner and setting `CORVINT_SELF_HOSTED` stay owner steps. AFP-V0-427 to AFP-V0-429
share no seed between runners or with the hosted image, cache no test result, and add no
isolation; the seed root needs no owner step, because the first refresh creates it.
AFP-V0-507 builds no mixed-scope receipt: `corvint affected --path` never evaluates HEAD plus
the named paths with a dirty overlay declared as uncertainty, because such a receipt would mix
bytes HEAD does not pin into HEAD's graph and needs a new scope kind that every consumer of the
`corvint-affected-plan` profile would have to learn before it could tell the overlay's units
from pinned ones; until then a consumer that ignored the overlay could under-select. The refusal
with its two exact recovery commands is the 1.0 behaviour (V1-2818).

## Failure modes

Git unavailable or the worktree status exceeds its bound: fail closed with
`unsupported-affected-status`. No commits: `unsupported-affected-revision`. Source walk exceeds
`affected.MaxWalkEntries`, a plugin returns a non-canonical unit, or the walk accepts a source file
whose path no unit can name: `unsupported-affected-graph`.
An unreadable or invalid `--playwright-config`, or a Playwright graph that cannot be built:
`unsupported-playwright-affected`; dynamic but readable project/config semantics remain a typed
`UNKNOWN` plan with `FULL_RELEVANT_SUITE`, not a command failure.
Exhausting an admitted-directory sub-bound instead skips only that subtree and reports
`go:included-directory-walk-bounded` at `UNKNOWN` scope; it is not a graph refusal.
A dirty Tasks store projection path that no unit names is task state in `nonSource`, not an
unknown entry (AFP-V0-088). A dirty path owned by no plugin, or a changed unit (one that owns a changed path) with no
selectable test in any language (AFP-V0-020): the plan widens to `UNKNOWN` scope rather than
narrowing; the packages that name a dirty path by literal are added, never substituted for the
widening (AFP-V0-021). A potentially deleted or renamed-away Go path widens and selects its
package, or the importers of a package that is gone, rather than claiming no dependency path
(AFP-V0-004). An unreadable subtree:
`unsupported-affected-graph`, never a silently smaller graph. Worktree or HEAD changed during
compilation: `unsupported-affected-drift`. A `--base` that is not a full commit id:
`invalid-arguments`; one that is not a commit here: `unsupported-affected-revision`; a range diff
over its bound: `unsupported-affected-status`. `--require-clean` over a worktree with any status
entry: `unsupported-affected-mixed-worktree` (AFP-V0-045). In the fast tier every one of these, a plan the
script cannot read, a module-level Go frontier (including an invalid AFP-V0-023 read-scope
declaration or AFP-V0-037 binary-exec declaration), a dirty root module definition, or an empty
selection over a non-empty diff, or a repository the selector cannot index (AFP-V0-012) runs the
full `./...` command instead of a narrowed one, so the
worst case of `make gate-affected` is the cost of `make go-test`, never a skipped package.
The advisory cost-drift report (AFP-V0-040) abstains, with its reason in the job summary, on a
shard that did not succeed, fewer or more outcome artifacts than shards, an unreadable, failed,
empty, malformed, structurally invalid or unfinished package stream, or an invalid cost table; it
never fails CI on a finding, and a failure of any of its own steps, including the tool's build,
abstains with its reason in the job summary rather than failing the job.
In full CI, an unusable test-split allow-list, slice file or cost table keeps every package whole,
and a split package's catch-all slice runs every top-level test that no named slice names
(AFP-V0-041). A split package whose slice outcomes are failed, unterminated or not one per slice,
logs that are not one per declared shard, or a slice file that splits a package into more slices
than the declared shards, refuses the cost refresh and makes the drift report abstain
(AFP-V0-041 (8)). Shard logs that are not one per declared shard, or a shard log without any
terminal package outcome, refuse slice generation and replay (AFP-V0-041 (5)). The sharded PR
driver refuses, before execution, a placement that does not put every package or slice of the
runtime universe on exactly one shard (AFP-V0-048). Slice generation and replay combine a split
package's per-slice outcomes; any other repeated outcome, a test outside the slice its log ran,
or a slice that ends twice still refuses (AFP-V0-049).

Queue-backstopped PR narrowing failure modes (decision 0431): absent queue rule or unreadable
rules API, an allowlist miss or a new frontier force FULL; missing selection at queue time
produces `INCOMPLETE`; a queue timeout shorter than `CI` fails entries. Disabling the queue
forces FULL through AFP-V0-028. AFP-V0-026 retains the lingering control-plane status limit.
Late failures eject PRs; rerun passes classify flakes without changing the queue failure.
Artifact listing failure forces FULL; threshold trips and a reviewer-confirmed `CHANGE_CAUSED`
miss disable narrowing as AFP-V0-030 requires. Hosted auto-merge command compatibility is
`NOT_VERIFIED`, and net runner cost and ready-to-merge effects are `NOT_OBSERVED`.

`--path` failure modes (AFP-V0-094/095): a non-canonical value or an unsupported combination is
`invalid-arguments`, and so is a separated `--path` value that begins with `-`; a dirty worktree,
or an ignored untracked file the list names or the graph reads, is
`unsupported-affected-mixed-worktree`, never a plan that mixes bytes HEAD does not pin into HEAD's
graph; a listed path no language claims widens to `UNKNOWN`
rather than selecting nothing at `BOUNDED`.

The `--path` dirty-worktree refusal names its two recovery commands with HEAD's full commit id
(AFP-V0-507); it still plans nothing. Lexicon narrowing (AFP-V0-505, AFP-V0-506): a unit whose
only way to a dirty path was a closing tag or a bare `..` spelt `..\\` is no longer selected for
it; any literal, scope, import or root-locating call that can reach the path still selects it.

`--playwright-env` failure modes (AFP-V0-382 to AFP-V0-387): a repeated or invalid name, or the
flag without `--playwright-config` or with `--snapshot`, is `invalid-arguments`; an unrecorded
variable, an empty value, an undefined value under a `channel`, an escaped literal, an
unmodelled expression or a possible writer keeps the EXECUTION unknown with a detail
that names the variable. A caller-supplied value that differs from the runtime's is the caller's
observation and is visible in `plan.environment`; it moves no test, because the executable is on
the EXECUTION axis. AFP-V0-389/404 fail closed: any unobserved load, workspace package, invalid
tsconfig, unmodelled marker or disagreement between the profile and the adapter keeps the
existing frontier. AFP-V0-405 to AFP-V0-413 fail closed: an unmodelled dotenv form, `.vault`
file, unreadable file or unmodelled line keeps the EXECUTION unknown and names the site or line,
and a non-literal tag closure (including an escaped or reflective route to a tag, an ambiguous
module extension or a dependency outside the bound source set), a reported tag the sources do not
hold or an unevaluable pattern keeps the omission unverified with its units named. A consumer still cannot read closure depth from an
`impact` receipt; only the help says so (AFP-V0-096).

Graph cache failure modes (AFP-V0-390 to AFP-V0-392): a `--cache` inside the repository, a
`.git` directory (in any case, or reached through a case-insensitive or firmlinked path) or the
repository's actual Git or common directory, a Git directory that cannot be named, or an
unsupported combination, is `invalid-arguments`; a missing, damaged, forged, foreign, stale or
incompletely stamped entry, a FIFO or other non-regular entry, a cache directory replaced by a
link into the repository or not private to the user, an unwritable cache directory, an
unreadable or replaced executable or an unsupported platform is a full build with the same
receipt, never a refusal and never a reused graph; an observation that cannot be pinned,
including one with a transient read failure, writes no entry. For the AFP-V0-436 default
location every one of these, a placement refusal included, is an uncached call with the same
receipt and no stderr (AFP-V0-438); `affected cache` reports it as `UNAVAILABLE`. `--paths-from` refusals are `invalid-arguments`
(AFP-V0-395).
Self-hosted shard runner (AFP-V0-420 to AFP-V0-422): an offline or busy box leaves the routed
shards queued for up to GitHub's 24-hour queue limit, and the operator recovers by unsetting
`CORVINT_SELF_HOSTED`, cancelling the queued run and starting a new one; a registered runner stays
reachable by an approved fork pull request until it is removed; host drift fails the verification step with the named prerequisite; a
self-hosted run makes the cost-drift report abstain. A missing, unsafe or unreadable host-local seed
(AFP-V0-427) leaves a cold cache with a notice; a refresh above the cap removes the seed; a corrupt
seed may fail that runner's shards or pass silently, and the operator deletes the seed root on any
doubt (AFP-V0-429). A test that fills the per-runner `TMPDIR` (AFP-V0-430) fills the box's root disk
rather than its memory, and the next job on that runner removes it; a removed runner's directory
stays until the operator deletes it. A link, foreign owner or non-directory at `/var/tmp/corvint-ci`
fails the verification step before anything is removed.

Weekly flake report (AFP-V0-530): a failed artifact listing fails the run with `::error` rather
than reporting a quiet week; a record that could not be downloaded, is not `corvint-ci-flaky/0`, has
no `flaky.json` or exceeds 1 MiB is named under `Not read`, and the counts are a lower bound; a shard
index at or above `FLAKE_REPORT_SHARDS` is not listed, and the operator raises the variable when the
AFP-V0-441 matrix grows; a failed issue write fails the run and the job summary still carries the
report; a refused pin leaves the issue unpinned, and the summary says so; GitHub expires the records
after 90 days, so a window never exceeds that. Failed-shard typed reasons (AFP-V0-531): an
unreadable stream yields a section saying so; a package whose output carries several markers gets
the first matching reason in the order test-failures, build-failed, timeout, signal, panic; a
stream truncated by the runner lists the unfinished packages and `no package-level fail event`;
`failed.sh` never changes the shard's status, so an error in it only loses the section.

Compatibility and drift: the provider bundle grammar is consumed, not redefined; if
`go-live-test-provider-v0.md` changes its pattern grammar or bound, `providerMaxPackagePatterns`
and this spec change in the same commit. Unresolved decisions: whether a future composer consumes
this wire directly or the provider bundle grows a signed selection field; which trigger (editor
task, pre-commit hook) invokes the command; whether non-Go projections are wanted.

Rollout: the command ships in the experimental Go candidate; no adapter calls it. Rollback: delete
`cmd/corvint/affected.go`, its test, the help entries, this spec, and its README/INDEX rows; for
the fast tier alone, delete `script/gate-affected.sh`, `script/gate-affected_test.sh`, `tools/gate-affected-select`, the
`gate-affected` and `gate-affected-test` targets, and inline `GO_TEST_COMMAND` back into `go-test`,
which leaves `make gate` byte-for-byte as it was. Also delete `RangePaths` and `DecodeNameList` in
`internal/liveverify/affected/dirty.go` when the range form goes.
Promotion or kill: promote only after the LPCV-V0 composer accepts this wire or replaces it; kill
if a shadow run over 200 historical commits shows any selected-set miss against the full suite that
the plan did not mark `UNKNOWN`. The fast tier may become a push gate only after that same shadow
run passes; until then it is an everyday narrowing whose fallback is the full run.

Protected workflow/ruleset status: **VERIFIED** (2026-09-19). The repository workflow and literal
pins alone do not protect their own control plane; decision 0320 establishes the admission on the
free plan with AFP-V0-016 and ruleset 23699808, whose settings and observed runs are recorded in
`tools/corvint-pr-tests/README.md`. Decision 0390 removes the ruleset's bypass actor and adds
`doc-gates`; that ruleset change is not yet observed. For queue-backstopped PR narrowing,
AFP-V0-028/029 replace the historical PASS prerequisite; hosted enablement is `NOT_OBSERVED`.
The following closed runtime describes historical replay, not queue-backstopped PR execution.
The runtime environment is an allowlist with exact recorded bytes, a fixed absolute Go PATH,
`/usr/bin/cc`, `GOENV=off`, `LANG=C`, `LC_ALL=C`, `TZ=UTC`, and exclusively owned HOME/TMP/cache
under `/tmp/corvint-pr-tests-runtime`. An existing runtime path is refused; owned runtime state is
removed on return; cleanup failure is reported with a nonzero exit. Output/runtime paths are
resolved through their nearest existing ancestors before creation and rechecked as real external
directories, so a symlinked parent cannot create inside the tested tree. Historical rows use that same fixed layout and reset HOME/TMP/GOCACHE immediately before each test invocation.
Stdout is capped at 128 MiB and stderr at 8 MiB; overflow immediately cancels the process group
and invalidates execution. Hosted log preview is capped at 1 MiB/256 KiB. Planner/selector drift
at launch falls back to full; Go/compiler/Git/environment drift fails safely. The frozen identity
records the test Git executable path, digest and version, because tests run Git and its
version-specific output changes their outcomes. The driver's own Git reads and the `go test`
process both keep `GIT_GRAFT_FILE=/dev/null`, so tests inherit the graft refusal in every mode. The `go test`
environment differs only by setting `advice.graftFileDeprecated=false` through `GIT_CONFIG_COUNT`,
since naming a graft file makes current Git print deprecation advice into the combined output tests
read (build log 2026-10-01-ci-shadow-graft-advice). Freeze ignores grafts;
row reuse binds the execution source/environment and raw plan/audit hashes as well as outcomes.

Container control operations have a two-minute deadline within an 80-minute launcher ceiling;
the test operation keeps its 75-minute bound. Docker daemon logging is disabled and inspected,
so PID1 file descriptors cannot bypass the retained-output bound. Both historical and PR
container checkouts use `/work/checkout`; process capture cannot bypass limits via io.Copy.
Run export requires only its actual selection/execution/Go outputs, not historical row files.

## Bounded documentation CI exception (2026-09-29)

The owner requested implementing a qualified README presentation path after PR #348. The separate
[Documentation CI V0](documentation-ci-v0.md) contract defines the only documentation-specific
exception to AFP-V0-013/014's root race invocation. It requires trusted-base source, exact merge
binding, a closed document scope, independently reviewed source-bound consumer qualification and
retained documentation checks. All other mandatory checks and AFP-V0-016 control-plane acceptance
remain. (accepted (decision 0431); amended by decision 0431) It does not alter this planner's
UNKNOWN scope or enable general Go selection; AFP-V0-028/029 govern queue-backstopped general
Go narrowing without the historical campaign. Missing or stale DCI qualification runs FULL.
